e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
, we fix a map

\exp_{w_{e}^{\pm}}^{S}:(U(T_{w_{e}^{\pm}}\tilde{S}),0)\to(U_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S}),w_{e}^{\pm}),\\
U(T_{w_{e}^{\pm}}\tilde{S})\subset T_{w_{e}^{\pm}}\tilde{S},\quad U_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S})\subset\tilde{S}

(8.2)

that biholomorphically maps a neighborhood
U(T_{w_{e}^{\pm}}\tilde{S})
of the
origin in the tangent space onto a neighborhood
U_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S})
of
the lift of the node
w_{e}
, satisfies
d\exp_{w_{e}^{\pm}}^{S}(0)=\operatorname{Id}
, and
varies smoothly with
S
. The family of maps

\{\exp_{w_{e}^{\pm}}:e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)\}
is fixed for the
rest of this book. Whenever we choose complex coordinates

z_{\pm}:(U_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S}),w_{e}^{\pm})\to(\mathbb{C},0)

in neighborhoods of the node we assume that they are compatible with

\exp_{w_{e}^{\pm}}^{S}
. That is,
z_{\pm}
is the composition of

\exp_{w^{\pm}}
with a linear map on the tangent space. On an open
neighborhood

U_{\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}}\subset\mathcal{M}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}

of
\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
, there is a projection map

\pi_{\Gamma}:U_{\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}.

such that curves in a fiber
\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(S)
are obtained by
gluing the interior nodes of
S
as follows. (See, for example,
Siebert
[
79
, Proposition 2.4]
in the closed case; the open
case is similar.)

Definition 8.5
.

Given a nodal sphere
S
of type
\Gamma
, a

collection of gluing parameters
is a
tuple

\delta=(\delta_{e})_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{-}(\Gamma)},\quad\delta_{e}\in T_{w^{+}_{e}}\tilde{S}\otimes T_{w^{-}_{e}}\tilde{S}.

The
curve corresponding to a gluing parameter

\delta
is
defined by

S^{\delta}:=(S\backslash\cup_{e}U^{\prime}_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S}))/\sim,\\
z_{+}\sim z_{-}\Leftrightarrow(\exp^{S}_{w^{+}_{e}})^{-1}(z_{+})\otimes(\exp^{S}_{w^{-}_{e}})^{-1}(z_{-})=\delta_{e}.

(8.3)

Here
U^{\prime}_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S})\subset\tilde{S}
is an open neighborhood of

w_{e}^{\pm}
such that the boundary
\partial U^{\prime}_{w_{e}^{\pm}}(\tilde{S})
is
identified to
\partial U_{w_{e}^{\mp}}(\tilde{S})
(with reversed orientation)
by the equivalence relation
\sim
. Thus, for any node
w_{e}
, the
relation
\sim
identifies the pair of annuli

U_{w^{+}_{e}}(\tilde{S})\backslash U^{\prime}_{w^{+}_{e}}(\tilde{S})\xrightarrow{\sim}U_{w^{-}_{e}}(\tilde{S})\backslash U^{\prime}_{w^{-}_{e}}(\tilde{S}).

(8.4)

The resulting annulus in
S^{\nu}
is called
\operatorname{Neck}_{e}(S^{\nu})
, and

\operatorname{Neck}(S^{\nu}):=\cup_{e}\operatorname{Neck}_{e}(S^{\nu})\subset S^{\nu}.

(8.5)

The gluing construction maps a neighborhood of zero in the space of
gluing parameters to a neighbourhood of
S
in the fiber

\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(S)
. Curves in the neighborhood
U_{\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}}
of

\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
possess a
gluing parameter

function for every smoothed node

\delta=(\delta_{e})_{e}:U_{\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}}\to\prod_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)}T_{w^{+}_{e}}\tilde{S}\otimes T_{w^{-}_{e}}\tilde{S},\quad S^{\delta}\mapsto\delta.

Remark 8.6
.

Suppose that at a node
w_{e}
we choose a framing

{\operatorname{fr}}:T_{w^{+}_{e}}\tilde{S}\otimes T_{w^{-}_{e}}\tilde{S}\to\mathbb{C}
as in
(
4.19
) or complex coordinates

z_{+}:(\tilde{S},w^{+}_{e})\to(\mathbb{C},0),\quad z_{-}:(\tilde{S},w^{-}_{e})\to(\mathbb{C},0).

Then the gluing parameter
\delta_{e}
may be identified with a complex
number.

Remark 8.7
.

(Identifications between nearby
curves) Consider
a rational nodal curve
[S]\in\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
and
a smooth curve

[S^{\prime}]\in\mathcal{M}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}
in the
neighborhood of
S
. The complement of nodes
S^{\circ}
can be
identified to subsets of the curve
S^{\prime}
as follows. First, suppose
that
S^{\prime}
is obtained by gluing
S
. That is,

S^{\prime}\in\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(S)
. For a vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, let

S^{\prime}(v)\subset S^{\prime}
be the subset corresponding to the component

S_{v}\subset S
including the necks
\operatorname{Neck}_{e}(S^{\prime})
,
v\in e
. The
subsets
S^{\prime}(v)
cover
S^{\prime}
. By the gluing construction, there are
natural inclusions

i_{S_{v},S^{\prime}}:S^{\prime}(v)\to S_{v}\subset S.

(8.6)

Next, suppose
S^{\prime}
is not in
\pi_{\Gamma}^{-1}(S)
. Consider
a neighborhood
U_{\Gamma,S}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
of
S
on which the
universal curve
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}
can be trivialized, so that the
variation of the complex structure is given by a map

U_{\Gamma,S}\to\mathcal{J}(\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}),\quad m\mapsto j(m)

(8.7)

for which
j(m)
is
m
-independent in the neighborhood of special
points. The trivialization of the universal curve
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}
gives
a diffeomorphism
\phi:\pi_{\Gamma}(S^{\prime})\to S
. The maps

i_{S_{v},S^{\prime}}
are defined as compositions

i_{S_{v},S^{\prime}}:=\phi\circ i_{\pi_{\Gamma}(S^{\prime})_{v},S^{\prime}}.

If a sequence
S_{\nu}
of curves converges to
S
, then the images

i_{S_{v},S_{\nu}}(S_{\nu}(v))
exhaust the complement of nodes
S_{v}^{\circ}

as
\nu\to\infty
.

Remark 8.8
.

(Uniqueness of
identifications) The identifications between regions of nearby
curves are unique in the following sense. Suppose a sequence

[S_{\nu}]\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}
converges to a curve

[S]
. For any node
e=(v_{+},v_{-})
in
S
, the identification in the
neck region

i_{S_{v_{\pm}},S^{\nu}}:\operatorname{Neck}_{e}(S^{\nu})\to S_{v_{\pm}}

(8.8)

is uniquely determined by the choice of the exponential map
(
8.2
). On the complement of the neck region, let

\phi_{\nu},\phi_{\nu}^{\prime}:S^{\nu}\backslash\operatorname{Neck}(S^{\nu})\to S

be two possible identifications given by trivializations of the
universal curve. The maps

\phi_{\nu}
,
\phi_{\nu}^{\prime}
have the same image, and the maps

\phi_{\nu}^{\prime}\circ\phi_{\nu}^{-1}
converge to the identity uniformly
in all derivatives.

Remark 8.9
.

(Identifications of nearby treed disks)
The identifications between nearby nodal spheres extend to treed disks. Let
C
be a stable treed disk of type
\Gamma
with surface part
S
and treed part
T
.

(a)

(Glued treed disk)
For a disk node
e\in\operatorname{Edge}^{0}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma)
of zero length,
\ell(e)=0
, a
gluing parameter
at the node
w_{e}\in C
is an element

\delta_{e}\in(T_{w_{e}^{+}}\partial S\otimes T_{w_{e}^{-}}\partial S)_{\leq 0}\subset T_{w_{e}^{+}}S\otimes T_{w_{e}^{-}}S

where the orientation on

T_{w_{e}^{+}}\partial S\otimes T_{w_{e}^{-}}\partial S
is derived from
the orientation on the boundaries of components of
S
. Given
gluing parameters

\delta:=(\delta_{e})_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)\cup\operatorname{Edge}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}^{0}(\Gamma)}

for all nodes in the surface component
S
, a glued surface

S^{\delta}
is defined exactly as in (
8.3
). The glued
treed curve is
C^{\delta}:=S^{\delta}\cup T
, that is, the treed
part in
C^{\delta}
is the same as that of
C
.

(b)

(Domain identifications) Let
C
be a treed curve of type

\Gamma
, and let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a treed disk type obtained by
collapsing a subset of interior edges

e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
and zero length boundary edges

e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{0}(\Gamma)
. Let
C^{\prime}=S^{\prime}\cup T^{\prime}
be a
treed disk of type
\Gamma^{\prime}
that is close to
C
. For any surface
component
S_{v}\subset C
corresponding to a vertex

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, there is a subset
S^{\prime}(v)\subset S^{\prime}
and an
identification

i_{S_{v},S^{\prime}}:S^{\prime}(v)\to S_{v}

exactly as in (
8.6
).

Next, we identify punctured neighborhoods of interior nodes to
annuli in nearby curves. These identifications are needed for stating
the
(Thin cylinder convergence)
for tropical nodes in the definition of Gromov convergence. We make these identifications only for interior nodes since all tropical nodes are interior nodes.

Informally, we may restate the definition below as follows: A sequence
of annuli converging to a node
w
is obtained by gluing neighborhoods

U_{w^{+}}
,
U_{w^{-}}
of the nodal lifts
w^{+}
,
w^{-}
. Given
holomorphic coordinates in a neighborhood of the nodal
w^{+}
,
w^{-}
, a
sequence of centered annuli converging to a node
w
is given by
gluing neighborhoods of
w^{+}
,
w^{-}
that have the same radius.

Definition 8.10
.

(Annuli converging to a
node) Let
C_{\nu}
be a sequence of treed disks converging to a
limit curve
C
for which the arguments

\frac{\delta_{e}(C_{\nu})}{|\delta_{e}(C_{\nu})|}
of the gluing parameters
converge for all interior edges
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
. Let
w\in C
be an interior node
corresponding to the edge
e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
.

(a)

A sequence of
annuli
A_{\nu}\subset C_{\nu}
converges to a
node
w
in
C
if there are open neighborhoods

U_{w^{\pm}}\subset S_{{v}_{\pm}}
of
the lifts
w^{+}
,
w^{-}
of
w
such that

A_{\nu}=i_{S_{v_{+}},C_{\nu}}^{-1}(U_{w^{+}})\cap i_{S_{v_{-}},C_{\nu}}^{-1}(U_{w^{-}}).

We say that the sequence of annuli
A_{\nu}
is obtained by

gluing the node
w
.

(b)

(Centered annuli converging to a node) Suppose the node

w\in C
is equipped with complex coordinates

z_{\pm}:(U_{w^{\pm}},w^{\pm})\to(\mathbb{C},0)

on neighborhoods
U_{w^{\pm}}\subset C_{{v}_{\pm}}
of its lifts

w^{+}
,
w^{-}
.
A
sequence of centered annuli converging to the node
w
is
a sequence of parametrized annuli

A_{\nu}:=[-l_{\nu}^{\prime}/2,l_{\nu}^{\prime}/2]\times\mathbb{R}/2\pi\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow C_{\nu}

for which there exists
\epsilon>0
such that

A_{\nu}=i_{S_{v_{+}},C_{\nu}}^{-1}(\{|z_{+}|\leq\epsilon\})\cap i_{S_{v_{-}},C_{\nu}}^{-1}(\{|z_{-}|\leq\epsilon\}),

and the map

A_{\nu}\xhookrightarrow{i_{S_{v_{\pm}},C_{\nu}}}C_{v_{\pm}}\xrightarrow{z_{\pm}}\mathbb{C}\quad\text{is equal to}\quad(s,t)\mapsto\exp(\mp(s+it)-(l_{\nu}+i\theta_{\nu})/2).

Here
e^{-(l_{\nu}+i\theta_{\nu})}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the neck length
parameter for the curve
C_{\nu}
resulting from the choice of
coordinates
z_{\pm}
.

The following definition describes rescalings of the target spaces
required in the statement of convergence of components of the broken
map. We recall from Definition
3.65
that a translation
is a way of identifying a subset of a neck-stretched manifold
X^{\nu}

to a subset of the broken manifold
\mathfrak{X}
. The set of translations is
parametrized by
\nu B^{\vee}
where
B^{\vee}
is the dual complex of
the neck-stretching. A translation sequence for a tropical graph
consists of a translation sequence corresponding to each of the
vertices of the tropical graph that are compatible with the edge
directions of the tropical graph.

Definition 8.11
.

(Translation sequence for a tropical graph)

Suppose
\Gamma
is a
pre-tropical graph (as in Definition
4.6
). A

\Gamma
-translation sequence
consists of a collection of
sequences

\{t_{\nu}(v)\in\nu B^{\vee}\}_{\nu},\quad v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)

such that the following conditions hold :

(a)

(Polytope) For each vertex
v

t_{\nu}(v)\in\nu P(v)^{\vee}\subset\nu B^{\vee},

(8.9)

and for any polytope
P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}
,
P_{0}\supset P(v)
,

d_{B^{\vee}}(t_{\nu}(v),\nu P_{0}^{\vee})\to\infty\quad\text{as}\quad\nu\to\infty.

(b)

(Direction) For any node
e
between vertices
v_{+}
,
v_{-}
,
there is a sequence
l_{\nu}(e)\to\infty
such that

t_{\nu}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}(v_{+})=\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}.

(8.10)

Remark 8.12
.

The existence of a translation sequence
\{t_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
on a
pre-tropical graph
\Gamma
implies that
\Gamma
is a tropical
graph, since, for any
\nu
,

\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)\ni v\mapsto\tfrac{t_{\nu}(v)}{\nu}\in\nu P(v)^{\circ}

is a tropical vertex position for
\Gamma
.

Finally, we define the notion of convergence of a sequence of maps in neck-stretched manifolds to a broken map in a broken manifold.

Definition 8.13
.

(Gromov convergence,
multiple cuts) Let
C_{\nu}=S_{\nu}\cup T_{\nu}
be a sequence of stable treed disks of type
\Gamma_{0}
. A sequence of holomorphic maps

u_{\nu}:(C_{\nu},\underline{z}_{\nu})\to X^{\nu}

(Gromov) converges
to a broken map

u:C=(S\cup T)\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{P}}
of type
\Gamma
and framing
{\operatorname{fr}}
if the following are satisfied.

(a)

(Convergence of domains) The sequence
of treed disks
C_{\nu}
converges to
C
and for any tropical node

w_{e}
of the limit map
u
the arguments

\frac{\delta_{e}(C_{\nu})}{|\delta_{e}(C_{\nu})|}
of the gluing parameters
converge to a limit. Using the fixed holomorphic exponential map
(
8.2
), let
S_{\nu}(v)\subset S_{\nu}
be the subset
corresponding to a vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, and let

i_{v,\nu}:=i_{S_{v},C_{\nu}}:S_{\nu}(v)\to S_{v},\quad S_{\nu}(v)\subset S_{\nu},

be embeddings from (
8.8
) whose images

i_{v,\nu}(S_{\nu}(v))
exhaust the complement of nodes
S_{v}^{\circ}

as
\nu\to\infty
.

(b)

(Convergence of maps) There is a

\Gamma
-translation sequence
\{t_{\nu}(v)\}_{v,\nu}
such that for
any irreducible surface component
S_{v}\subset S
, the sequence of
maps

S_{v}^{\circ}\supset i_{v,\nu}(S_{\nu}(v))\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}(v)}(u_{\nu}\circ i_{v,\nu}^{-1})}\mathfrak{X}_{P(v)}

converges in
C^{\infty}_{{\operatorname{loc}}}(S_{v}^{\circ})
to

u_{v}:S_{v}^{\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P(v)}
. The map

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}(v)}:X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}(v)}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P(v)}
is defined in
(
3.59
). For each boundary edge
e
in
\Gamma_{0}
, the
map
u_{\nu}|T_{e,\nu}
on the treed segment converges to a (possibly
broken) treed segment in
u
.

(c)

(Thin cylinder convergence) For a
node
w
in
C
corresponding to a tropical edge

e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\operatorname{trop}}(\Gamma)
, let

z_{\pm}:(U_{w^{\pm}},w^{\pm})\to(\mathbb{C},0)

be matching coordinates (see Definition
4.14
following
(
4.22
)) on neighborhoods

U_{w^{\pm}}\subset S_{{v}_{\pm}}
of the nodal point which respect
the framing
{\operatorname{fr}}_{e}
. Let

A(l_{\nu}):=[-l_{\nu}/2,l_{\nu}/2]\times S^{1}\subset S_{\nu}

be a sequence of centered annuli converging to the node
w
, see
Definition
8.10
. Then the sequence

x_{\nu}:={\mathbb{e}}^{-{\tfrac{1}{2}}(t_{\nu}(v_{+})+t_{\nu}(v_{-}))}u_{\nu}(0,0)\in\mathfrak{X}_{P(e)}

converges to a limit
x_{0}
, and the components
u_{v_{\pm}}
of the
broken map are asymptotically close to

z_{\pm}\mapsto z_{\pm}^{\mathcal{T}(e)}x_{0}

in the sense of Remark
4.26
.

Remark 8.14
.

(On the uniqueness of limits)

The uniqueness of the limit, when it exists, is explained in Theorem

8.2
. The Gromov limit does not depend on the various
choices made in this section. In particular, the proof of uniqueness
only depends on the
(Convergence of domains)

and
(Convergence of maps)
. The latter item
depends on the maps
i_{v,\nu}
which are identifications between
complements of neighborhoods of nodes in nodal curves, and complements
of corresponding neck regions in nearby smooth curves. Different
choices of these maps converge in the limit as pointed out in Remark

8.8
.

We remind the reader that we should not expect to prove that the
Gromov limit is independent of perturbation data which includes the
choice of Donaldson divisors, since the definition of
pseudoholomorphic maps is dependent on these choices. Later, in
Chapter
10
, we will prove that different choices of
perturbation data produce homotopy-equivalent Fukaya algebras.

8.2
Horizontal convergence

In this section, we discuss a notion of convergence for a sequence of
points in neck-stretched manifolds, called
horizontal
convergence
, that is useful in the proof of convergence for
breaking maps. It has the feature that any sequence of points

\{x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}\}_{\nu}
in the family of neck-stretched manifolds

X^{\nu}
has a subsequence that horizontally converges.

Definition 8.15
.

(Horizontal convergence) A sequence of points
x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}

horizontally converges
to a point
x\in X_{P}
for a polytope

P\in\mathcal{P}
if

—

x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}}
for all
\nu
, and the sequence

\pi_{P}^{\nu}(x_{\nu})
converges to
x
, where

\pi_{P}^{\nu}:X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}}\to X_{P}
is the projection map

X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}}\to X^{\nu}_{P}
from (
3.37
) composed with the
inclusion
X^{\nu}_{P}\to X_{P}
,

—

and for any subsequence of
\{x_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
, the above
condition is not satisfied for any polytope
P_{0}\supset P
.

We present some results on horizontal convergence of sequences:

Lemma 8.16
.

For any sequence
x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}
, there exists a subsequence of

\{x_{\nu_{k}}\}_{k}
that converges horizontally. There is a unique
polytope
P\in\mathcal{P}
for which the subsequence
\{x_{\nu_{k}}\}_{k}

converges horizontally in
X_{P}
.

Beweis.

Recall from (
7.12
) that there is a projection to the dual
polytope
\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}:X^{\nu}\to\nu B^{\vee}
for all
\nu
.
There is a polytope
P\in\mathcal{P}
such that, after passing to a
subsequence,
(x_{\nu})_{\nu}
satisfies the equation

\sup_{\nu}d(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(x_{\nu}),\nu P^{\vee})<\infty,\quad d(P_{0}^{\vee},\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(x_{\nu}))\to\infty\kern 5.0pt\forall P_{0}\supset P.

(8.11)

It is then clear that a subsequence of
(x_{\nu})_{\nu}
and the
polytope
P
satisfy the conclusions of the Lemma.
∎

The following Lemma relates horizontal convergence to a property of translation sequences.

Lemma 8.17
.

Suppose
P\in\mathcal{P}
is a polytope with
\operatorname{codim}(P)>0
, and suppose

x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}
is a sequence of points, and

t_{\nu}\in\nu P^{\vee}
is a sequence of translations such that

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}x_{\nu}
converges in
\mathfrak{X}_{P}
. Then, the following are
equivalent:

(a)

The sequence
x_{\nu}\in X^{\nu}
converges horizontally in
X_{P}
.

(b)

For any
P_{0}\supset P
(that is,
P
is a proper face of
P_{0}
),
d(t_{\nu},\nu P_{0}^{\vee})\to\infty
.

Beweis.

Firstly, we observe that the convergence of the translated sequence

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}x_{\nu}
implies that

d(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(x_{\nu}),\nu P^{\vee})
is uniformly bounded, and
that
(\pi_{P}(x_{\nu}))_{\nu}
converges to a limit in
X_{P}
. The
condition in (
b
) then implies that a
(x_{\nu})_{\nu}

horizontally converges in
X_{P}
. The converse is similar and is left
to the reader.
∎

The next result may be seen as the horizontal convergence version of
the Arzela-Ascoli theorem.

Lemma 8.18
.

Suppose
C
is a connected curve and
u_{\nu}:C\to X^{\nu}
is a
sequence of differentiable maps satisfying

\sup_{\nu}\lVert{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
. There exists a
subsequence of maps
\{u_{\nu_{k}}\}_{k}
and a polytope
P\in\mathcal{P}

such that

(a)

for all
z\in C
the sequence
u_{\nu_{k}}(z)
converges
horizontally in
X_{P}
,

(b)

and there is a sequence of translations
t_{\nu}\in\nu P^{\vee}
such that

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}u_{\nu}
converges uniformly in compact sets to a map

u:C\to\mathfrak{X}_{P}
.

For the subsequence
\{u_{\nu_{k}}\}_{k}
, the polytope
P
is unique.

Beweis.

First, assume that
C
is compact. The proof of Lemma

8.16
carries over. Indeed, by the uniform bound on

\sup_{\nu}\lVert{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, the sequence

d(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(u_{\nu}(z_{0})),P^{\vee})
is uniformly bounded for
a fixed point
z_{0}\in C
iff the sequence

d(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(u_{\nu}(z)),P^{\vee})
is uniformly bounded for
any
z\in C
. The result also holds for non-compact curves since
they are exhausted by a sequence of compact curves.

For (
b
), we view
u_{\nu}
as mapping to the

P
-cylinder, since for any translation

t_{\nu}\in\nu P^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{P}
there is an embedding

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}:X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P},

see (
3.59
). We choose a sequence of translations

t_{\nu}\in t_{P}^{\vee}
so that

\pi_{\mathfrak{t}_{P}}\circ({\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}u_{\nu})(z_{0})
is

\nu
-independent. The horizontal convergence of
u_{\nu}
and the
uniform bound on the derivatives of
u_{\nu}
imply that for any
compact set
K\subset C
, the images
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}u_{\nu}(K)
are
contained in a compact set of
\mathfrak{X}_{P}
. The Arzela-Ascoli theorem
then implies that the sequence
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}\tilde{u}_{\nu}
converges.
∎

8.3
Breaking annuli

In the next proposition, called the
breaking annulus lemma
, we
consider a sequence of maps on annuli with small areas and bounded
Hofer energy that converge to a pair of maps on punctured disks, the
maps on punctures being asymptotic to trivial cylinders. The main
conclusion of the proposition is that there is a trivial cylinder

u_{\operatorname{triv}}
to which the sequence of annuli is asymptotically
close. That is, the distance from
u_{\operatorname{triv}}
decays exponentially
towards the middle of the cylinders. Furthermore, if the limit
punctured disks lie in the components
\mathfrak{X}_{P_{+}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{P_{-}}
of
the broken manifold, where
P_{+}
,
P_{-}
are polygons in the polyhedral
decomposition
\mathcal{P}
, then, the trivial cylinder
u_{\operatorname{triv}}
lies in

\mathfrak{X}_{P_{\cap}}
, where
P_{\cap}:=P_{+}\cap P_{-}
, or in other words, the
direction
\mu
of
u_{\operatorname{triv}}
is in
\mathfrak{t}_{P_{\cap}}
.

In the proof of convergence of maps in neck-stretched manifolds to a
broken map in Section
8.4
, the breaking annulus
lemma is used to show that a sequence of maps on annuli (as in the
previous paragraph) converges to a neighborhood of a node in a broken
map; in particular, to show that the
matching condition
is
satisfied at nodes. The matching condition says that on the punctured
neighborhoods of both lifts of the node, the map is asymptotic to the
same trivial cylinder. In this setting, both punctured neighborhoods
are asymptotic to
u_{\operatorname{triv}}
. The asymptotic closeness of the annuli to

u_{\operatorname{triv}}
also implies that the convergence of the annuli satisfies
the
(Thin cylinder convergence)

property. (Horizontal matching), which is one of the conclusions of
the breaking annuli result, is a sub-condition of the node-matching
condition.

The breaking annulus lemma also gives an approximation of the
difference in the translation sequences in the convergence of maps on
the two ends of the annuli (see (
a
)), which is
used in constructing the tropical structure of the limit map in the
proof of Gromov convergence in Section
8.4
. This
approximation follows from the fact that the maps on the annuli are
well-approximated by trivial cylinders of the same length. The
breaking annulus lemma is analogous to results in Parker
[
65
, Lemmas
5.11, 5.13]
.

In the statement of the breaking annulus lemma, the hypothesis on
small area is replaced by a bubble-free condition (Definition

8.19
). For any
L>0
, we denote by

A(L):=[-\tfrac{L}{2},\tfrac{L}{2}]\times S^{1}\cong\{z\in\mathbb{C}\ :\ |z|\in[e^{-L/2},e^{L/2}]\}

the cylinder with length
L
equipped with the product metric.

Abbildung 8.2:
A sequence of annuli converging to a neighborhood of a node in a broken map.

Definition 8.19
.

(Bubble-free) A sequence of maps

u_{\nu}:A(l_{\nu})\to X^{\nu},\quad l_{\nu}\to\infty

satisfying
\sup_{\nu}\lVert du_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
is

bubble-free
if, for any
P\in\mathcal{P}
, there is no non-constant map

v:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to X_{P}
that is a limit of a subsequence of
reparametrized maps

\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu}(\cdot-r_{\nu})\quad\text{with }r_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R},\left|r_{\nu}\pm\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}\right|\to\infty.

Proposition 8.20
.

(Breaking annulus lemma) Let
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
be a cylindrical almost
complex structure on
\mathfrak{X}
for which Hofer energy is monotonic (see
Lemma
7.21
), and suppose that for any
\nu
the almost
complex structures
J_{0}^{\nu}
is obtained by gluing
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
on the
necks. There are constants
0<\rho<1
,
c>0
such that the
following hold. For a sequence
l_{\nu}\to\infty
, let

u_{\nu}:A(l_{\nu})\to X^{\nu}

be a sequence of
J_{0}^{\nu}
-holomorphic maps satisfying

(1)

\sup_{\nu}E_{{\operatorname{Hof}}}(u_{\nu})<\infty
,

\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}\times S^{1},\nu}|{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}(z)|<\infty,
where the
domain annuli

A(l_{\nu}):=[-\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2},\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}]\times S^{1}
are
equipped with the product metric.

(2)

The sequence
\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
is bubble-free.

(3)

There exist polytopes
P_{+},P_{-}
such that the sequence of maps

u_{\nu}(\cdot\pm\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})
converges horizontally in

X_{P_{\pm}}
.

(4)

There exists a sequence of translations

t_{\nu}^{\pm}\in\nu P^{\vee}_{\pm}
such that the sequence

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{\pm}}u_{\nu}(\cdot\pm\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})
converges in

C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}
to

u_{\pm}:\mathbb{R}_{\mp}\times S^{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P_{\pm}},

and the map
extends holomorphically over
\mp\infty
, possibly after passing to a finite cover in the orbifold case.

Then, there exists
\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{P_{\cap},\mathbb{Z}}
,
P_{\cap}:=P_{+}\cap P_{-}
for
which the following hold after passing to a subsequence of

\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
.

(a)

The sequence

t_{\nu}^{+}-t_{\nu}^{-}-\mu l_{\nu}\in\mathfrak{t}_{P_{\cap}}
is uniformly
bounded.

(b)

(Horizontal matching) The points

(\pi_{P_{+}}\circ u_{+})(-\infty))
,

(\pi_{P_{-}}\circ u_{-})(+\infty)
lie in

X_{P_{\cap}}\subset\overline{X}_{P_{\pm}}
and

(\pi_{P_{+}}\circ u_{+})(-\infty))=(\pi_{P_{-}}\circ u_{-})(+\infty)
.

1
1

1

The point

(\pi_{P_{\pm}}\circ u_{\pm})(\mp\infty)\in\overline{X}_{P_{\pm}}
is
defined, see (
4.26
).

(c)

(Asymptotic decay) Let
\xi_{\nu}
be a section defined by
the relation

u_{\nu}=\exp_{u_{\nu,\operatorname{triv}}}\xi_{\nu},\quad u_{\nu,\operatorname{triv}}(s,t):=e^{\mu(s+it)}u_{\nu}(0,0).

There exists
l\geq 0
and a subsequence such that

\lvert\xi_{\nu}(s,t)\rvert\leq c(e^{\rho(s-\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})}+e^{\rho(-s-\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})}),\quad\forall s\in\left[-\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}+l,\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}-l\right].

(8.12)

Lemma 8.21
.

Let
l_{\nu}\to\infty
be a sequence. Suppose

u_{\nu}:[0,l_{\nu}]\times S^{1}\to X^{\nu}
is a sequence of maps that
converges horizontally in
P_{0}\in\mathcal{P}
, and for which there is a
sequence of translations
t_{\nu}\in\nu P_{0}^{\vee}
such that

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}u_{\nu}
converges uniformly on compact subsets to a map

u:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P_{0}}
, whose projection

\pi_{P_{0}}\circ u
has a removable singularity at the infinite end
with
u(\infty)\in X_{P_{1}}\subset\overline{X}_{P_{0}}
for some

P_{1}\subseteq P_{0}
. Then, there is a subsequence
r_{\nu}\to\infty

such that

u_{\nu}(r_{\nu},\cdot):S^{1}\to X^{\nu}

converges horizontally in
P_{1}
.

Beweis.

The removal of singularity for
u
implies that there exists a point

x_{0}
in the
P_{1}
-cylindrical end of
\mathfrak{X}_{P_{0}}
and an element

\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{P_{1},\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(\cup_{P\supset P_{1}}\mathfrak{t}_{P})

such that

d(u(s,t),e^{\mu(-s-it)}x_{0})\leq ce^{-|s|}\quad(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1},

see Remarks
4.24
and
4.26
.

Then, there is a sequence
r_{\nu}\to\infty
and translations

t_{\nu}\in\nu P_{0}^{\vee}
such that

{\operatorname{d}}(u_{\nu}(r_{\nu},\cdot),{\mathbb{e}}^{t_{\nu}}e^{\mu(-r_{\nu}-it)}x_{0})\to 0\quad\text{ as }\nu\to\infty,

where
{\mathbb{e}}^{t_{\nu}}
maps a subset of
\mathfrak{X}_{P_{0}}
to
X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}_{0}}

(see (
3.61
)). It follows that
u_{\nu}(r_{\nu},\cdot)

horizontally converges to the constant map
\pi_{P_{1}}(x_{0})
in

X_{P_{1}}
. (Note that the values of
t_{\nu}
are not relevant to
the conclusion of horizontal convergence since

t_{\nu}\in\nu P_{0}^{\vee}\subset i\mathfrak{t}_{P_{0}}
, and for the purpose
of horizontal convergence in
X_{P_{1}}
, we quotient the map by

T_{P_{1},\mathbb{C}}
which contains
T_{P_{0},\mathbb{C}}
as a subtorus.)
∎

Lemma 8.22
.

Suppose
l_{\nu}\to\infty
and

u_{\nu}:[0,l_{\nu}]\times S^{1}\to X^{\nu}_{{\tilde{P}}}\subset X^{\nu}
is a
sequence of maps satisfying hypotheses
(1)
,
(2)

of Proposition
8.20
. Furthermore, assume that

\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu}
converges on compact subsets to a constant map

u:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}\to X_{P}
with value
x_{0}\in X_{P}
. Then, there is a compact subset
K\subset X_{P}
that
contains the image of
(\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu})
for all

\nu
.
2
2

2

For any
\nu
, there is a natural inclusion

X^{\nu}_{P}\to X_{P}
, see Lemma
3.52
and Definition

3.53
.

Beweis.

The Lemma is proved by applying the annulus Lemma
8.24

on a compact subset of
X_{P}
. The intervals

I_{\nu}:=d_{\nu B^{\vee}}(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(u_{\nu}([0,l_{\nu}]\times S^{1}),\nu P^{\vee})\subseteq[0,\infty]

are connected, and the the conclusion in the Lemma is equivalent to

\sup_{\nu}\operatorname{length}(I_{\nu})<\infty.

(8.13)

Let

\tau:=d(\pi_{\operatorname{NCone}_{P^{\vee}}B^{\vee}}\circ u,P^{\vee})=d(\pi_{\operatorname{NCone}_{P^{\vee}}B^{\vee}}(x_{0}),P^{\vee}),

(8.14)

so that
\tau\in I_{\nu}
for all
\nu
. Let us assume that
(
8.13
) does not hold. Consider any
\kappa>0
. If
(
8.13
) does not hold, after passing to a subsequence,

\tau+\kappa\in I_{\nu}\quad\forall\nu.

For each
\nu
, let
s_{\nu}\in[0,l_{\nu}]
be the least value for
which there is a point
z_{\nu}=(s_{\nu},\theta_{\nu})
in the domain of
u_{\nu}

d_{\nu B^{\vee}}(\pi_{\nu B^{\vee}}(u_{\nu}(z_{\nu})),\nu P^{\vee})=\tau+\kappa.

The minimality of

(8.16)

Because the limit

\tau

, we

. We also have

,

. By

. We now arrive

.
By (

3

converges to a pair

∎

Proof of Proposition

.

In Step 1 we will find a polytope

.

4

4

(8.18)

Automatically

.

Claim 8.23
.

The sequence

Beweis.

Since

.

Step 2
:
Determining the edge direction
\mu
.

In this step we read off the direction
\mu
of the edge from the topology
of the cylinders
u_{\nu}
and show that it is equal to
the directions of the limit maps at the nodal point. By Step 1, the
images
\pi_{P}(u_{\nu}(A(l_{\nu}-2L)))
are contained in a compact subset

K
of
X_{P}
. Choose a squashing area form
\omega_{\aleph}
on

that
is a symplectic form on
K
; such a form exists by Lemma

7.26
. The
\omega_{\aleph}
-area of
\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu}
is
uniformly bounded, since

\int_{A(l_{\nu}-2L)}(\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu})^{*}\omega_{\aleph}\leq E_{\operatorname{Hof}}(\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu})\leq E_{\operatorname{Hof}}(u_{\nu}),

where the last inequality is by Proposition
7.28
. By
the annulus lemma for compact symplectic manifolds (Proposition

8.25
), the annuli
\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu}
converge to
a pair of disks connected by an interior node

x_{0}\in X_{P}
. Therefore, for some
L_{1}>0
, the images

\pi_{P}(u_{\nu}(A(l_{\nu}-L_{1})))
lie in a neighborhood

B_{\epsilon}(x_{0})\subset X_{P}
. Consider a trivialization

B_{\epsilon}(x_{0})\times T_{P,\mathbb{C}}\simeq\pi_{P}^{-1}(B_{\epsilon}(x_{0}))\subset\mathfrak{X}_{P}.

of
\pi_{P}:\mathfrak{X}_{P}\to X_{P}
. Viewing the target space of
u_{\nu}

as the product
B_{\epsilon}\times T_{P,\mathbb{C}}
, the homotopy class

(u_{\nu})_{*}[A(l_{\nu})]\in\pi_{1}(T_{P})
corresponds to an element

\mu_{\nu}\in\mathfrak{t}_{P,\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{P,\mathbb{Z}}
be
defined so that
u_{-}
is asymptotically close to
a trivial cylinder of direction
\mu
at
\infty
in the sense of Remark

4.26
. Therefore
\mu_{\nu}=\mu
for large

\nu
. Similarly
u_{+}
is also asymptotically close to a trivial
cylinder of direction
\mu
at
-\infty
.

Step 3
:
The sequence of twisted
maps

\overline{u}_{\nu}:A(l_{\nu})\to\mathfrak{X}_{P},\quad(s,t)\mapsto e^{-\mu(s+\frac{l_{\nu}}{2}+it)}({\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{-}}u_{\nu})

converges to a pair of disks connected at an interior point.

Because the derivatives of the maps
(\overline{u}_{\nu})_{\nu}
are uniformly
bounded, we expect the Gromov-Floer limit of the sequence to consist
of two disks connected by a path of spheres. Each of the components
is obtained by a sequence of rescalings of the cylinder
of the form
(s,t)\mapsto(s+s_{\nu},t)
for some
sequence
s_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}
. The bubble-free condition rules out spheres
in the limit, leading to the conclusion of Step 3.
The proof is standard, except for the fact that we need to choose an
appropriate notion of symplectic area.

The first component in this Gromov-Floer limit of
\overline{u}_{\nu}
is a
disk
\overline{u}_{-}
which is a twisted version of
u_{-}
. This is seen as
follows. The convergence of
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{-}}u_{\nu}
to
u_{-}
implies
that the sequence
\overline{u}_{\nu}(\cdot-\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})
converges to

\overline{u}_{-}:=e^{-\mu(s+it)}u_{-}.

(8.21)

The image of
\overline{u}_{-}
is compact in
\mathfrak{X}_{P}
since
u_{-}
is
asymptotically close to the
\mu
-cylinder

(s,t)\mapsto e^{\mu(s+it)}
. Since
\pi_{P}\circ u_{-}
extends over

\infty
, the same is also true for
\overline{u}_{-}
. We denote

\overline{x}_{0}:=\overline{u}_{-}(\infty)\in\mathfrak{X}_{P}
.

Next, we finish the description of the Gromov-Floer limit. Choose
any taming symplectic form
\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}
defined in a neighborhood

U^{\prime}_{\overline{x}_{0}}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{P}
of
\overline{x}_{0}
. Let

U_{\overline{x}_{0}}\Subset U^{\prime}_{\overline{x}_{0}}
be a smaller open neighborhood
whose closure is contained in
U^{\prime}_{\overline{x}_{0}}
. Let
\kappa>0
be any
constant. Since
\overline{u}_{\nu}(\cdot-\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})
converges to

\overline{u}_{-}
, there exists a constant
r_{0}
, and a sequence
r_{\nu}

such that

\overline{u}_{\nu}([\tfrac{-l_{\nu}}{2}+r_{0},r_{\nu}]\times S^{1})\subset\overline{U}_{\overline{x}_{0}}\\
\text{and }\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}(u_{\nu},[\tfrac{-l_{\nu}}{2}+r_{0},r_{\nu}]\times S^{1})\leq\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}(\overline{u}_{-},[r_{0},\infty)\times S^{1})+\kappa.

(8.22)

We assume that for each
\nu
,
r_{\nu}
is the maximum value
satisfying the above condition, and therefore

r_{\nu}+\frac{l_{\nu}}{2}\to\infty
. Applying the annulus Lemma
for compact manifolds (Proposition
8.25
) to the
sequence of maps

u_{\nu}|([\tfrac{-l_{\nu}}{2}+r_{0},r_{\nu}]\times S^{1})
with target
space
(\overline{U}_{\overline{x}_{0}},\omega_{{\tilde{P}}})
, we conclude that the sequence
of annuli converges to a pair of disks
(\overline{u}_{-},\overline{u}_{+})
. Here, we
note that the first map is the same as the map in
(
8.21
). The second map

\overline{u}_{+}:(-\infty,L_{1})\times S^{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P}

is the limit of rescaled maps

\overline{u}_{\nu}^{+}(s,t):=\overline{u}_{\nu}(\cdot+r_{\nu}):[\tfrac{-l_{\nu}}{2}-r_{\nu},\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}-r_{\nu}]\times S^{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P},

where

L_{1}:=\lim_{\nu}(\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}-r_{\nu})\in(0,\infty].

(8.23)

Proposition
8.25
also implies that the images of
the components
\overline{u}_{-}
and
\overline{u}_{+}
connect at the nodal point,
that is,
\overline{u}_{-}(\infty)=\overline{u}_{+}(-\infty)
. Finally,
L_{1}
is
finite, because otherwise, the sequence
\overline{u}_{\nu}(\cdot+r_{\nu})

converges to a sphere in
\mathfrak{X}_{P}
, contradicting the
bubble-free assumption.

We have shown that the limit of the twisted maps is a pair of disks

(\overline{u}_{-},\overline{u}_{+})
. Since

L_{1}=\lim_{\nu}(\frac{l_{\nu}}{2}-r_{\nu})
is finite, after truncating
the domain cylinders by a
\nu
-independent amount, we may replace

r_{\nu}
by
\frac{l_{\nu}}{2}
. The limit
\overline{u}_{+}
will be altered
by a domain reparametrization and
\overline{u}_{+}(-\infty)=\overline{u}_{-}(\infty)

continues to hold.

Part (
a
) of the Proposition is now proved as
follows. Both sequences of maps

\overline{u}_{\nu}^{+}(s,t):=e^{-\mu(s+it)}({\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{-}-\mu l_{\nu}}u_{\nu}(s+\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2},t))

and
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{+}}u_{\nu}(\cdot+\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})
converge on

\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\times S^{1}
, the former by our proof and the latter by
the hypothesis. At the point
(s,t)=(0,0)
the sequences

\overline{u}_{\nu}^{+}(s,t)
and
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{+}}u_{\nu}(\cdot+\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})

differ by a translation by
e^{t_{\nu}^{+}-t_{\nu}^{-}-\mu l_{\nu}}
. Since both sequences of points converge, we conclude that
the limit

\delta:=\lim_{\nu}(-\mu l_{\nu}-t_{\nu}^{-}+t_{\nu}^{+})

exists (which proves (
a
)) and that

\overline{u}_{+}(s,t)=e^{\delta}e^{-\mu(s+it)}u_{+}(s,t).

Step 4
:
Proof of the decay estimate.

We have shown that after truncating the domain cylinders by a

\nu
-independent amount, say
L
, the sequence of the twisted maps

\overline{u}_{\nu}
converges to a pair of disks
(\overline{u}_{-},\overline{u}_{+})
, and the
images of the maps lie in a compact set
\overline{U}_{\overline{x}_{0}}
with a
taming symplectic form
\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}
. The sequence of domains can
truncated again by a finite amount to ensure that

\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}(\overline{u}_{\nu})<\hbar
, since the
\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}
-area on the
sequence of cylinders converges to the
\omega_{{\tilde{P}}}
-area of the pair
of disks
(\overline{u}_{-},\overline{u}_{+})
. We apply the annulus lemma for
compact manifolds (Proposition
8.24
) to the maps

\overline{u}_{\nu}:A(l_{\nu}-2L)\to(\overline{U}_{\overline{x}_{0}},\omega_{{\tilde{P}}})\subset\mathfrak{X}_{P}.

The decay estimate for the twisted maps
\overline{u}_{\nu}
implies the
asymptotic decay estimate (
8.12
) for
u_{\nu}
required by
the Proposition.

Step 5
:
Proof of Horizontal Matching and
P=P_{+}\cap P_{-}
.

By Step 1, the nodal lifts are mapped to on
X_{P}
, that is,

(\pi_{P_{\pm}}u_{\pm})(\mp\infty)\in X_{P}\subset\overline{X}_{P_{\pm}}.

Therefore at the nodal point,
u_{\pm}
intersects all the divisors

\overline{X}_{Q_{\pm}}
of
\overline{X}_{P_{\pm}}
which contain
\overline{X}_{P}
, that is,

P\subseteq Q_{\pm}\subseteq P_{\pm}
. Consequently,

\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{P}\backslash\cup_{Q\supset P}\mathfrak{t}_{Q}.

On the other hand
t_{\nu}^{+}-t_{\nu}^{-}\in\mathfrak{t}_{P_{\cap}}
, where

P_{\cap}:=P_{+}\cap P_{-}
. Since
t_{\nu}^{+}-t_{\nu}^{-}-\mu l_{\nu}
is
uniformly bounded, we conclude
\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{P_{\cap}}
. Therefore

P_{\cap}=P
. Horizontal matching now follows from Step 2, where we
showed that the sequence
(\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu})
converges in
X_{P}
to
pair of annuli connected at a node. This finishes the proof of the
breaking annulus lemma (Proposition
8.20
).
∎

The proof of the breaking annulus lemma was based on the following results (Propositions
8.24
and
8.25
) on compact symplectic manifolds.

Proposition 8.24
.

(Annulus lemma on compact
manifolds
[
55
, Lemma 4.7.3]
)
Suppose
(X,\omega_{X})
is a compact symplectic manifold with a tamed
almost complex structure
J
. There exists constants
0<\rho<1
,

\hbar>0
,
c>0
such that the following holds for any

J
-holomorphic map
u:A(\ell)\to X
with
E(u)\leq\hbar
. For

x=u(0,0)
, there is a map

\xi:A(\ell-1)\to T_{x}X\ \text{ such that }\ u=\exp_{x}\xi

on
A(\ell-1)
and

\lvert\xi(s,t)\rvert\leq c(e^{\rho(s-\ell)}+e^{\rho(-s-\ell)}),\quad\forall s\in[-\ell+1,\ell-1].

(8.24)

The constants
\rho
,
\hbar
,
c
depend continuously on
J
with respect to the
C^{2}
-topology.

Proposition 8.25
.

(Convergence of long
cylinders) Suppose
(X,\omega_{X})
is a compact symplectic manifold
with a tamed almost complex structure
J
. Let

u_{\nu}:A(l_{\nu})\to X
be a sequence of holomorphic cylinders
with a uniform bound on
\lVert du_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}

and the
\omega
-areas of
u_{\nu}
. Furthermore, the sequence

\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
satisfies the bubble-free condition: For any
sequence
r_{\nu}
with
|r_{\nu}\pm l_{\nu}/2|\to\infty
, if a
subsequence
of
\{u_{\nu}(\cdot-r_{\nu})\}_{\nu}
converges to a limit

v:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to X
, then
v
is a constant map. After
passing to a subsequence,
u_{\nu}(\cdot\pm\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})

converges in
C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}
to

u_{\pm}:\mathbb{R}_{\mp}\times S^{1}\to X,

the map
u_{\pm}
extends holomorphically over
\mp\infty
, and

u_{-}(\infty)=u_{+}(-\infty)
.

Beweis.

This Proposition is proved as part of the “bubbles connect” result
in
[
55
, Proposition 4.7.1]
.
∎

We give an annulus lemma for sequences of holomorphic strips in

X^{\nu}
whose boundaries lie on the Lagrangian submanifold
L
. The
result is simpler than the breaking annulus lemma since the strips
converge to a nodal disk in the complement of the relative divisors,
and not a tropical node. The result is stated for maps whose domains
are strips, defined as

A_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\ell):=[-\tfrac{\ell}{2},\tfrac{\ell}{2}]\times[0,1]

for any
\ell>0
. The result is a boundary version of the “bubbles
connect” result in McDuff-Salamon
[
55
, Proposition 4.7.1]
.
However, since the manifold
X_{P_{0}}
has cylindrical ends, we need to
use a Hofer energy bound.

Proposition 8.26
.

(Annulus lemma with boundary)

Suppose that the almost complex structures
\mathfrak{J}_{0}\in\mathcal{J}^{\operatorname{cyl}}(\mathfrak{X})

and
J_{0}^{\nu}\in\mathcal{J}^{\operatorname{cyl}}(X^{\nu})
satisfy the conditions in the
statement of Proposition
8.20
. There are
constants
0<\rho<1
,
c>0
such that the following hold. For a
sequence
l_{\nu}\to\infty
, let

u_{\nu}:A_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(l_{\nu})\to X^{\nu},\quad u_{\nu}(\partial A_{\nu})\subset L

be a sequence of
J_{0}^{\nu}
-holomorphic strips satisfying the
following.

(1)

\sup_{\nu}E_{{\operatorname{Hof}}}(u_{\nu})<\infty
,

\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,1],\nu}|{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}(z)|<\infty.

(2)

The sequence
\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
satisfies a disk bubble-free
condition: For any sequence
r_{\nu}
with

|r_{\nu}\pm l_{\nu}/2|\to\infty
, if a subsequence
of
\{u_{\nu}(\cdot-r_{\nu})\}_{\nu}
converges to a limit
v:\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to X
, then
v
is a constant map.

(3)

The sequence of maps
u_{\nu}(\cdot\pm\frac{l_{\nu}}{2})

converges in
C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}
to a limit

u_{\pm}:\mathbb{R}_{\mp}\times[0,1]\to X_{P_{0}},

and the map
extends holomorphically over
\mp\infty
.

Then,
u_{+}(-\infty)=u_{-}(+\infty)\in L
.

Beweis.

There is a compact subset
K\subset X_{P_{0}}
that contains the
image all the strips
\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
. Indeed,
L\subset X_{P_{0}}
is
compact and the images of the strips are contained in a

\sup_{\nu}\lVert du_{\nu}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
-radius of
L
. By Lemma

7.26
, there is a squashing area form
\omega_{\aleph}
that
is a symplectic form on
K
. The
\omega_{\aleph}
-area of the strips

u_{\nu}
are uniformly bounded. Therefore, the target space may be
seen as a compact symplectic manifold, and the proof is analogous to
the “bubbles connect” result for long cylinders in

[
55
, Proposition 4.7.1]
.
∎

8.4
Proof of convergence for breaking maps

The arguments used in the proof of convergence of breaking maps are
similar to those used in the proof of Gromov compactness for stable
maps. The new features include a more refined analysis of sequences of
long cylinders that converge to nodes of the limit broken maps. The
decay estimate on such cylinders coming from the breaking annulus
lemma (Proposition
8.20
) allows us to prove

(Thin cylinder convergence)
, and also prove
the existence of a tropical graph, that is, the graph underlying the
limit map is realizable (see Definition

4.6
) in the dual complex. The stabilizing divisor
plays a role in the proof, since, the choice of perturbation data
implies that any non-constant limit component has a stable
domain.

Proof of Theorem
8.2
.

Step 1
:
Domain components for
the limit map.

The sequence of domains
(C_{\nu},z_{\nu})

converges to a stable treed nodal curve
(C,z)
modelled on a tree

\Gamma
. The perturbation maps
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\nu}
converge to a
perturbation datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\infty}=(J_{\infty},F_{\infty})
defined on

C
. In the next few steps, we will show that there are no
additional domain components in the limit map.

In the rest of the proof, we focus on the convergence of maps on
irreducible surface components. The proof of convergence of gradient
trajectories on treed components does not involve any technical
difficulties arising from multiple cutting, and are left to the
reader.

Step 2
:
Boundedness of
derivatives.

In this step, we show a bound on the derivatives of the sequence of
maps by ruling out bubble trees with unstable domains in the limit.
In particular, we will show that for any
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, the
derivative of

u_{v,\nu}:=u_{\nu}\circ i_{v,\nu}^{-1}:C_{v}^{\circ}\to X^{\nu}

is uniformly bounded for all
\nu
. The norm on the derivative is
with respect to the cylindrical metric on
X^{\nu}
. On the domain we
use a metric on
C^{\circ}
that is cylindrical (or strip-like) in the
punctured neighborhood of nodal points and on the neck regions in

C_{\nu}
.

Assume by way of contradiction that the derivatives are not
uniformly bounded. We will construct a sequence of rescaled maps
which converges to a constant map with positive derivative, which
cannot exist. After passing to a subsequence, there exists a
sequence of points
z_{\nu}\in C_{v}^{\circ}
and a point

z_{\infty}\in C_{v}
for some
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
such that

z_{\nu}\to z_{\infty},\quad|{\operatorname{d}}u_{v,\nu}(z_{\nu})|\to\infty.

We first carry out the proof assuming that
z_{\infty}
is not a nodal
point and does not lie on the boundary
\partial C_{v}
, and thus

z_{\infty}\in C_{v}^{\circ}
. We apply Hofer’s lemma
8.27
to
the function
|{\operatorname{d}}u_{v,\nu}|
,
x=z_{\nu}
and the constant

\delta=|{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}(z_{\nu})|^{-1/2}
.

Lemma 8.27
.

(Hofer’s Lemma,
[
55
, Lemma 4.6.4]
)
Suppose
(X,d)
is a metric space,
f:X\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is a
continuous function, and
x\in X
,
\delta>0
are such that the
ball
B_{2\delta}(x)
is complete. Then there exists a positive
constant
\epsilon\leq\delta
and a point
\zeta\in B_{2\epsilon}(x)

such that

\sup_{z\in B_{\epsilon}}f(z)\leq 2f(\zeta),\quad\epsilon f(\zeta)\leq\delta f(x).

We obtain another sequence
\zeta_{\nu}\in C_{v}^{\circ}

converging to
z_{\infty}
, and a sequence of constants

\epsilon_{\nu}\to 0
such that

c_{\nu}:=|{\operatorname{d}}u_{v,\nu}(\zeta_{\nu})|\to\infty,\quad\sup_{z\in B_{\epsilon_{\nu}}}|{\operatorname{d}}u_{\nu}(z)|\leq 2c_{\nu},\quad c_{\nu}\epsilon_{\nu}\to\infty.

The rescaled maps are

\tilde{u}_{\nu}:=u_{v,\nu}((\cdot-\zeta_{\nu})/c_{\nu}):B_{\epsilon_{\nu}c_{\nu}}\to X^{\nu},

and
|d\tilde{u}_{\nu}|\leq 2
,
|d\tilde{u}_{\nu}(0)|=1
.

Each of the rescaled maps converges to a limiting map with domain
the affine line as follows. By Lemma
8.18
there is a
polytope
P\in\mathcal{P}
and a sequence of translations

t_{\nu}\in t_{P}^{\vee}
so that after passing to a subsequence, the
maps
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}\tilde{u}_{\nu}
converge in
C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}
to a
non-constant
J_{z_{\infty}}
-holomorphic limit
\tilde{u}:\mathbb{C}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P}
.

The limit map
\tilde{u}:\mathbb{C}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P}
in the previous paragraph is
asymptotic to a trivial cylinder at infinity: Hofer energy

E_{{\operatorname{Hof}}}(u_{\nu},C_{\nu})
is equal to area (Remark

7.19
) and therefore is uniformly bounded for all

\nu
. By monotonicity of Hofer energy (Lemma
7.21
), the
Hofer energy on a ball
E_{{\operatorname{Hof}}}(\tilde{u}_{\nu},B_{\epsilon_{\nu}c_{\nu}})
is
uniformly bounded. By Proposition
7.27
,

E_{\operatorname{Hof}}
is preserved in the neck-stretching limit (in a certain
sense), which implies that the quantity
E^{*}_{P,{\operatorname{Hof}}}(\tilde{u},\mathbb{C})
is
finite. By Proposition
7.1
, there exists

Q\subseteq P
and
\mu\in\mathfrak{t}_{Q}
such that at
\infty
,
\tilde{u}
is
asymptotically close to
z\mapsto z^{\mu}x_{0}
for some

x_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{Q}
.

Finally, we arrive at a contradiction by showing that the limit map
is constant. The domain reparametrizations
\phi_{v,\nu}
were
derived from the stable map compactification. It follows from the definition of the compactification there is at
most a single marked point
z_{i,\nu}
that is contained in each of
the regions
B_{\epsilon_{\nu}}(\zeta_{\nu})
. Therefore, the projection

\pi_{P}\circ\tilde{u}:\mathbb{C}\to X_{P}
either lies in the stabilizing
divisor
D_{P}
, or it has at most one intersection with the divisor

D_{P}
. Since
(\mathfrak{J}_{0},\mathfrak{D})
is a stabilizing pair for
\mathfrak{X}
, and the
perturbation
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
is adapted to
(\mathfrak{J}_{0},\mathfrak{D})
. Neither
possibility can happen if
\pi_{P}\circ\tilde{u}
is
non-constant. Therefore,
\pi_{P}\circ\tilde{u}
is constant, and so the
image of
\tilde{u}
lies in a single toric fiber
V_{P^{\vee}}
. The
image
\tilde{u}(\mathbb{C})
does not intersect torus-invariant divisors of

V_{P^{\vee}}
, and therefore
\tilde{u}
is a constant map.

We now consider the case that the sequence of points with increasing
derivatives converges to an interior nodal point
w_{e}
. The sequence

z_{\nu}
lies on the neck region

A_{\nu}:=\left[\tfrac{-l_{\nu}}{2},\tfrac{l_{\nu}}{2}\right]\times S^{1}\subset C_{\nu}

obtained by gluing the
node
w_{e}
, and
l_{\nu}\to\infty
as
\nu\to\infty
. Since

z_{\nu}
converges to the node
w_{e}
, there is a constant
\epsilon>0

such that the ball
B_{\epsilon}(z_{\nu})
is contained in
A_{\nu}
for all

\nu
. The proof in the preceding few paragraphs using Hofer’s
Lemma and a rescaling can now be applied to the maps

u_{\nu}|B_{\epsilon}(z_{\nu})
, because there are no marked points in

A_{\nu}
. We conclude that
|{\operatorname{d}}u_{v,\nu}|
is uniformly bounded for
all
\nu
.

Finally, if the sequence of points with increasing derivatives
converges to a point on the boundary of the domain, then the same
steps can be repeated to show the existence of a non-constant disk
bubble with unstable domain, leading to a contradiction. The removal
of singularities in this case is proved by Proposition

7.34
.

Step 3
:
Determining stable components of the limit map.

In Step 2 we showed that the derivatives of the maps
u_{v,\nu}
are
uniformly bounded, where
v
is any vertex of
\Gamma
. By Lemma

8.18
, there is a polytope
P(v)
and a sequence of
translations
t_{\nu}(v)\in\nu P(v)^{\vee}\subset i\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}^{\vee}

such that after passing to a subsequence, the maps

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}(v)}u_{v,\nu}
converge in
C^{\infty}_{{\operatorname{loc}}}
to a limit

u_{v}:C_{v}^{\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P(v)}
. By arguments as in Step 2,
the Hofer energy

E_{{\operatorname{Hof}}}(u_{v},C_{v}^{\circ})
is bounded. By removal of singularities
Proposition
7.1
,

—

u_{v}
has a removable singularity at the node
w
, or

—

in a punctured neighborhood of the node
w
,
u_{v}
is
asymptotic to a vertical cylinder, and this latter case can only
happen if
w
is an interior point of the domain.

We obtain a
J_{\infty}
-holomorphic map

u_{v}:C_{v}^{\circ}\to\mathfrak{X}_{P(v)}
that is adapted to the
stabilizing divisor
\mathfrak{D}_{P(v)}\subset\mathfrak{D}
.

For later use, we remark that the convergence continues to hold if
the sequence
t_{\nu}(v)
is replaced by a sequence
t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime}
for
which
\sup_{\nu}|t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime}-t_{\nu}(v)|<\infty
. In that case, after
passing to a subsequence, the limit
u_{v}
would be replaced by

e^{-t}u_{v}
, where
t:=\lim_{\nu}(t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime}-t_{\nu}(v))
.

Step 4 :

Determining the
tropical structure
and constructing translation sequences.

We have so far determined the domain treed disk of the limit, and
the limit map
u_{v}
corresponding to each vertex.
The construction above determines the polytope
P(v)\in\mathcal{P}
for
each component
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
of the limit. Furthermore, for
every
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
the map
u_{v}
is the limit of

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}(v)}u_{\nu}
where
t_{\nu}(v)\in\nu P^{\vee}
is a sequence
of translations.

First, we observe that all boundary edges are internal edges, and
are collapsed by the tropicalization morphism of (
4.13
).
Vertices
v\in\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma)
corresponding to disk
components map to a single vertex
v_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}
in the tropical graph
and the translation sequence
t_{\nu}(v_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
is the point

P_{0}^{\vee}
.

Edge directions of the tropical graph are determined using the breaking
annulus lemma. For any node
w_{e}
of
C
corresponding to an
interior node
e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
, choose a
complex coordinate

z_{e}^{\pm}:(U_{\pm},w_{e})\to(\mathbb{C},0)

(8.25)

in a neighborhood
U_{\pm}\subset C_{v_{\pm}}
of the lift
w_{e}^{\pm}

of the node, and let
A_{e,\nu}:=A(l_{\nu}(e))\subset C_{\nu}
be a
sequence of centered annuli converging to the neighborhoods
U_{+}
,

U_{-}
of the nodal lifts
w_{e}^{+}
,
w_{e}^{-}
(as in Definition

8.10
). Since there are no markings
z_{i,\nu}
on the
annuli
A_{e,\nu}
, the maps
u_{\nu}|A_{e,\nu}
are bubble-free in
the sense of Definition

8.19
. Indeed, if for
some
P\in\mathcal{P}
and some reparametrization of the domain, the
sequence of projections
\pi_{P}\circ u_{\nu}|A_{e,\nu}
converges to
a map
v:\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}\to X_{P}
, then
v
extends to a map to

\overline{X}_{P}
defined on an orbifold completion of
\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}
;
such a limit map
v
is constant since it either lies on the
stabilizing divisor
D_{P}
or does not intersect
D_{P}
at all.
Since the sequence of annuli
u_{\nu}|A_{e,\nu}
is bubble-free, the
breaking annulus lemma (Proposition
8.20
) is
applicable,
and we obtain a direction
\mathcal{T}(e)\in\mathfrak{t}_{P(e),\mathbb{Z}}

corresponding to the edge
e
. The breaking annulus lemma implies
that

\sup_{\nu}\{|t_{\nu}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}(v_{-})-\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}(e)|\}<\infty,

(8.26)

which is the
(Approximate Direction)
condition in Definition

8.28
.

The tropicalization of
\Gamma
is determined as follows. An edge

e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
is an internal (non-tropical) edge
exactly if
\mathcal{T}(e)
. In that case, the sequence is

|t_{\nu}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}(v_{-})|
is uniformly bounded for all

\nu
. Therefore, we can replace
t_{\nu}(v_{-})
by
t_{\nu}(v_{+})
(or
the other way around), and the convergence of Step 2 still
holds. By performing such replacements for all internal edges, we
may assume
t_{\nu}(v_{+})=t_{\nu}(v_{-})
for all

e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\operatorname{int}}(\Gamma)
. Therefore,
t_{\nu}

descends to the tropical graph
\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}}
and is an approximate

\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}}
-translation sequence.

Next, we obtain translation sequences and determine tropical vertex
positions. Since
\{t_{\nu}(v):v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}})\}
is an
approximate
\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}}
-translation sequence, by Lemma

8.30
, there is a
\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}}
-translation sequence

(t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime})_{v,\nu}
such that

\sup_{\nu,v}|t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime}-t_{\nu}(v)|<\infty.

(8.27)

The convergence of maps in Step 2 continues to hold if
t_{\nu}
is
replaced by
t_{\nu}(v)^{\prime}
. Any element of the translation sequence
gives a tropical vertex position map. Indeed, since

t_{\nu}^{\prime}=(t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v))_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}})},\quad t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v)\in\nu P(v)^{\vee}

satisfies the (Direction) condition
(
8.10
),

\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}})\ni v\mapsto\tfrac{t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v)}{\nu}

is a tropical vertex position map.

Step 5:

Finishing the proof of convergence.

To finish the proof of convergence it remains to show that the
collection of limit maps satisfy matching conditions at nodes, and
that
(Thin cylinder convergence)
is
satisfied at tropical nodes. We first consider disk nodes. For a
disk node
w_{e}\in C
corresponding to an edge

e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma)
with length
\ell(e)=0
, let

A_{e,\nu}\subset C_{\nu}
be a sequence of strips converging to the
node
w_{e}
. The maps
u_{\nu}|A_{e,\nu}
are disk bubble-free since
there are no markings
z_{i,\nu}
on
A_{e,\nu}
;
the reasoning is
identical to the one used following (
8.25
)
to
show that on the annuli converging to an interior node, maps are
bubble-free. Therefore, the boundary version of the annulus lemma
(Proposition
8.26
) is applicable on
u_{\nu}|C_{\nu}
and
we conclude that node matching holds, that is,

u_{v_{+}}(w_{e}^{+})=u_{v_{-}}(w_{e}^{-})\in L
.

Next, we determine matching coordinates for tropical nodes and
prove node matching using the breaking annulus Lemma. Matching
coordinates at a tropical node
w_{e}
,
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\operatorname{trop}}}(\Gamma)

are obtained by applying a correction to the holomorphic coordinate
chosen in (
8.25
). Multiplying a constant to the
coordinates
z_{e}^{+}
,
z_{e}^{-}
has the effect of adding a constant to
the sequence of neck length parameters

l_{\nu}(e)+i\theta_{\nu}(e)
. We recall that as a consequence of
(
8.26
) and (
8.27
) the

\Gamma
-translation sequence
t_{\nu}^{\prime}
satisfies

\sup_{\nu}\{|t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{-})-\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}(e)|\}<\infty.

Since
t_{\nu}^{\prime}
satisfies the (Direction) condition (
8.10
),
there is a sequence
l_{\nu}^{\prime}(e)\to\infty
for every

e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
such that

t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{-})=\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}^{\prime}(e),\quad\text{and}\quad\sup_{\nu}|l_{\nu}(e)-l_{\nu}^{\prime}(e)|<\infty.

Therefore, we can adjust the coordinates
z_{e}^{+}
,
z_{e}^{-}
by scalar
multiplication so that a subsequence of neck length parameters

l_{\nu}(e)+i\theta_{\nu}(e)
satisfies

\lim_{\nu}(t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v_{-})-\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}(e))=0,\quad\lim_{\nu}\theta_{\nu}(e)=0.

(8.28)

We will show that
(z_{e}^{+},z_{e}^{-})
are matching coordinates at the
node
w_{e}
for the broken map
u
. For interior non-tropical
nodes, we leave
(z_{e}^{+},z_{e}^{-})
from (
8.25
)
unchanged, and the following discussion is valid. To simplify
calculations, we use logarithmic coordinates in the neighborhood of
the node

C_{\pm}\supset U_{\pm}\backslash\{w_{e}^{\pm}\}\xrightarrow{\pm\ln z_{e}^{\pm}}\mathbb{R}_{\mp}\times S^{1}.

The annulus
A(l_{\nu}(e))
is then identified to the limit curve by
translations

A(l_{\nu}(e))\to U_{\pm},\quad(s,t)\mapsto s+it\mp{\tfrac{1}{2}}(l_{\nu}+i\theta_{\nu}).

By Step 2,

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t^{\prime}_{\nu}(v_{\pm})}u_{\nu}(s+it\pm{\tfrac{1}{2}}(l_{\nu}+i\theta_{\nu}))\to u_{v_{\pm}}\quad\text{in }C^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(U_{\pm}\backslash\{w_{e}^{\pm}\}).

(8.29)

We apply the breaking annulus lemma on the maps
u_{\nu}
on the
annuli
A(l_{\nu}(e))
. The resulting decay estimate together with
(
8.29
), (
8.28
) implies the convergence of
the sequence

{\mathbb{e}}^{-{\tfrac{1}{2}}(t^{\prime}_{\nu}(v_{+})+t^{\prime}_{\nu}(v_{-}))}u_{\nu}(0,0)\to x_{0}\quad\text{in }\mathfrak{X}_{P(e)}.

It follows that
u_{v_{\pm}}
are asymptotically close to the
cylinder
(s,t)\mapsto e^{\mathcal{T}(e)(s+it)}x_{0}
in the sense of

4.26
. We conclude that
z_{e}^{+}
,
z_{e}^{-}
are matching
coordinates at
w_{e}
and
(Thin cylinder
convergence)
is satisfied. Note that we have shown node matching
for all interior edges, both tropical and internal, in a unified
way. In case of an internal node
w_{e}
,
\mathcal{T}(e)=0
, and we have
shown that
u_{v_{\pm}}(\mp\infty)=x_{0}\in\mathfrak{X}_{P(v_{\pm})}.

Step 6
:
Uniqueness of the limit.

The limit of the domain curves is unique up to reparametrization,
because the limit is a stable curve. The identifications between
subsets of
C^{\nu}
to the limit curve are unique in the following
sense (see Remark
8.8
) : The neck regions in
C^{\nu}

are parametrized in a unique way, and the difference between any
two choices of identifications of the complement of the neck in

C^{\nu}
to
C
converge uniformly to identity as
\nu\to\infty
. Let
\Gamma
be the combinatorial type of the limit curve

C
. For every vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, the polytope
P(v)
in
the limit map is uniquely determined as follows: Suppose there is a
translation sequence

\{t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v)\in P^{\prime}(v)^{\vee}:v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)\}_{\nu}
for which
Gromov convergence holds. Then the property

d_{B^{\vee}}(t_{\nu}(v),P_{0}^{\vee})\to\infty\quad\forall P_{0}\in\mathcal{P},P_{0}\supset P^{\prime}(v)

of a translation sequence implies, by Lemma
8.17
, that
the maps
u_{\nu,v}
horizontally converge in
P^{\prime}(v)
. However,
P^{\prime}(v)=P(v)
because the
polytope of horizontal convergence is unique by Lemma
8.18
.

Translation sequences are well-determined up to uniformly bounded
perturbations as follows : Suppose
t_{\nu}
,
t_{\nu}^{\prime}
are two
distinct translation sequences, such that the sequence

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}}u_{\nu}
resp.
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{\prime}}u_{\nu}
converges to a broken
map
u
resp.
u^{\prime}
. Then for all vertices
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
,
there is a uniform bound

\sup_{\nu}|t_{\nu}(v)-t^{\prime}_{\nu}(v)|<\infty,

because both the sequences
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}(v)}u_{\nu}
,

{\mathbb{e}}^{-t_{\nu}^{\prime}(v)}u_{\nu}
converge pointwise in
C_{v}^{\circ}
. After
passing to a subsequence, we may assume that there exists a limit

t(v):=\lim_{\nu}t_{\nu}(v)-t^{\prime}_{\nu}(v).

Then, for each vertex
v
,
u_{v}=e^{t(v)}u_{v}^{\prime}
. Since
u_{v}
and

u_{v}^{\prime}
satisfy matching conditions at nodes we conclude that
t
is
an element of
T_{{\operatorname{trop}},\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
, which is the identity
component of
T_{\operatorname{trop}}(\Gamma)
. We have thus shown that the limit
is unique up to the action of
T_{{\operatorname{trop}},\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
.
∎

The proof of convergence of breaking maps used an
approximate translation sequence
, which was eventually refined to obtain a tranlation sequence for the convergence.

Definition 8.28
.

(Approximate translation sequence)

Suppose
\Gamma
is
a pre-tropical graph (as in Definition
4.6
). An
approximate
\Gamma
-translation sequence

consists of sequences
\{t_{\nu}(v)\in\nu P(v)^{\vee}\}_{\nu}
for
each
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
such that

—

(Approximate Direction) For any edge

e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{-}(\Gamma)
, there exists a sequence

l_{\nu}(e)\to\infty
such that

\sup_{\nu}(t_{\nu}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}(v_{+})-\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu})<\infty.

The differences appearing in the

(Approximate Direction)
condition will be
referred to later using the following notation:

Definition 8.29
.

On a tropical graph
\Gamma
define the
discrepancy
function
on any edge
e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
as

\operatorname{Diff}_{e}:\oplus_{\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)}\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}\to\mathfrak{t}_{P(e)}/\langle{\mathcal{T}(e)}\rangle,\quad(t_{v})_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)}\mapsto(t_{v_{+}}-t_{v_{-}})\mod\mathcal{T}(e).

Lemma 8.30
.

(From approximate to exact translation sequences)

Suppose
\Gamma
is a pre-tropical graph and
t_{\nu}
is an
approximate
\Gamma
-translation sequence. Then, after passing to a
subsequence, there is a
\Gamma
-translation sequence
\overline{t}_{\nu}

such that
|\overline{t}_{\nu}(v)-t_{\nu}(v)|
is uniformly bounded for all

\nu
,
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
. Consequently,
\Gamma
is a tropical
graph.

Beweis.

The
(Approximate Direction)
condition says
that the sequences of discrepancies
(\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t_{\nu}))_{\nu}
are
uniformly bounded. Via uniformly bounded adjustments to
t_{\nu}
, we
aim to make this quantity vanish for all edges.

We give an algorithm that transforms
t_{\nu}
into a bounded sequence

t_{\nu}^{k}\in\oplus_{\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)}\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}
, and will prove
later that
t_{\nu}-t_{\nu}^{k}
is an exact translation sequence. The
algorithm is as follows:

Step 1
:
Relativisation
.
In this step, we replace
t_{\nu}
by

t^{0}_{\nu}(v):=t_{\nu}(v)-\nu\lim_{\nu}(t_{\nu}(v)/\nu)\in\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}.

The limit in the right-hand side exists after passing to a
subsequence because the original translation sequences
t_{\nu}
lie
in
\nu B^{\vee}
and
B^{\vee}
is compact. For any

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
, the discrepancies across edges are preserved:

\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t_{\nu})=\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{0}_{\nu}).

(8.30)

Step 2
:
Subtracting fastest
growing sequences
.
By a sequence of further transformations, we will change
t^{0}_{\nu}

to a bounded sequence
t^{k}_{\nu}\in\mathfrak{t}
. At each step, the
sequence
t^{i}_{\nu}
is replaced by
t^{i+1}_{\nu}
defined as
follows. Choose a vertex
v_{0}\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
for which the rate
of increase of the sequence
|t^{i}_{\nu}(v_{0})|
is the maximum. That
is, for all
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
,

\lim_{\nu}|t^{i}_{\nu}(v)|/|t^{i}_{\nu}(v_{0})|
is finite. Such a vertex can
indeed be chosen, because after passing to a subsequence, the limit

\lim_{\nu}|t^{i}_{\nu}(v_{i})|/|t^{i}_{\nu}(v_{j})|
exists in
[0,\infty]
for
any pair of vertices. Now, define

r^{i}_{\nu}:=|t^{i}_{\nu}(v_{0})|,

and

t^{i+1}_{\nu}(v):=t^{i}_{\nu}(v)-r^{i}_{\nu}\lim_{\nu}\frac{t^{i}_{\nu}(v)}{r^{i}_{\nu}}\in\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}.

(8.31)

We stop the iteration when the sequence
t^{i}_{\nu}(v)
corresponding
to every vertex is bounded, and suppose the final sequence is
q
t^{k}_{\nu}
.

The process terminates in a finite number of steps. Indeed, notice
that
t^{i+1}_{\nu}(v_{0})=0
for all
\nu
. The number of vertices

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
for which
t^{i+1}_{\nu}(v)
vanishes is at least
one more than the number of vertices
v
for which
t^{i}_{\nu}(v)

vanishes.

The iterations of the algorithm preserve the discrepancies across
edges: For all tropical edges
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\operatorname{trop}}}(\Gamma)

\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i+1}_{\nu})=\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i}_{\nu}).

(8.32)

Indeed, (
8.31
) implies

\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i+1}_{\nu})=\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i}_{\nu})-r^{i}_{\nu}\lim_{\nu}\frac{\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i}_{\nu})}{r^{i}_{\nu}},

and the second term in the right-hand-side vanishes because

\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{i}_{\nu})
is uniformly bounded and
r^{i}_{\nu}\to\infty
as

\nu\to\infty
.

We claim that
t_{\nu}-t_{\nu}^{k}
is an exact translation sequence.
For all vertices
v
the (Polytope) condition (
8.9
)

(t_{\nu}-t_{\nu}^{k})(v)\in P(v)^{\vee}
is satisfied because

t_{\nu}(v)\in P(v)^{\vee}
and

t_{\nu}^{k}(v)\in\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}\simeq TP(v)^{\vee}
. The (Direction)
condition is satisfied because
\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t_{\nu})-\operatorname{Diff}_{e}(t^{k}_{\nu})=0

by (
8.30
) and (
8.32
). The last statement that

\Gamma
is a tropical graph follows from Remark

8.12
.
∎

8.5
Convergence for broken maps

In this section, we prove Theorem
8.3
on Gromov
compactness for broken maps. The limit map may have additional
components because of bubbling and consequently the tropical graph of
the limit map may have additional vertices. The tropical graph of the
limit map is related to the tropical graph of the maps in the sequence
by a
tropical edge collapse
relation defined below. We show that
such bubbling happens only in families whose dimension is at least
two, and so does not occur in the zero-dimensional moduli spaces we
use to define the Fukaya algebra.

Definition 8.31
.

(Collapsing edges
tropically) A

tropical edge collapse
is a morphism of tropical graphs

\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
that collapses a subset of
edges
\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma^{\prime})\backslash\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma)
in
\Gamma^{\prime}

inducing a surjective map on the vertex sets

\kappa:\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})\to\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma),

and satisfies the following conditions:

(a)

for any vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,

P(v)\subseteq P(\kappa(v))
; and

(b)

the edge direction is unchanged for uncollapsed edges, i.e. if

\mathcal{T}
,
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
are the edge direction functions for
\Gamma
,

\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
\mathcal{T}(\kappa(e))=\mathcal{T}^{\prime}(e)
for any uncollapsed
edge
e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.

Since the edge direction function
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
extends
\mathcal{T}
, we
often use the same notation for both. A tropical edge collapse

\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
is
trivial
if no edge is
collapsed, and
P_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)=P_{\Gamma}(\kappa(v))
for all
v
.

The definition of the tropical edge collapse morphism

\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
is meaningful even if
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a
pre-tropical graph (Definition
4.6
). This extension of
terminology is often used in proofs at points when the realizability
of
\Gamma^{\prime}
has not yet been ascertained.

Definition 8.32
.

(Tropical edge collapse for a disk type)
Let
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
be combinatorial types of treed disks that are equipped with a tropical structure given by tropicalization maps

{\operatorname{tr}}_{1}:\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma_{1,{\operatorname{tr}}},\quad{\operatorname{tr}}_{2}:\Gamma_{2}\to\Gamma_{2,{\operatorname{tr}}}.

An edge collapse morphism
\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma_{2}
is a
tropical edge collapse
if it is a lift of a tropical edge collapse map
\Gamma_{1,{\operatorname{tr}}}\to\Gamma_{2,{\operatorname{tr}}}
between the tropical graphs.

Example 8.33
.

In Figure
4.5
, collapsing the middle edge in
\Gamma_{2}
gives a tropical edge collapse morphism
\kappa:\Gamma_{2}\to\Gamma_{1}
. See Figure
8.1
for another example of a tropical edge collapse morphism.

Example 8.34
.

Figure
6.2
lists the types of broken maps whose gluing
has a certain fixed homology class. The tropical graphs in Figure

6.2
are labelled
\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{7}
. For every
even
i
there are edge collapse morphisms

\Gamma_{i}\to\Gamma_{i-1}
and
\Gamma_{i}\to\Gamma_{i+1}
.

8.5.1
Relative translations

In the definition of convergence of broken maps, the role of translation sequences is played by
relative translation sequences
, introduced in Definition
8.35
below.
Components of a relative translation correspond to maps of broken
manifolds that rescale the coordinates on cylindrical ends. For a
pair
Q\subseteq P
of polytopes, we recall that (
3.51
)
gives an embedding

i_{Q}^{{\tilde{P}}}:U_{Q}(\mathfrak{X}_{P})\to\mathfrak{X}_{Q}

(8.33)

where
U_{Q}(\mathfrak{X}_{P})\subset\mathfrak{X}_{P}
is the
Q
-cylindrical end of
\mathfrak{X}_{P}

from (
3.44
). If
Q=P
, then
U_{Q}(\mathfrak{X}_{P})=U_{P}(\mathfrak{X}_{P})=\mathfrak{X}_{P}

and the map in (
8.33
) is the identity map. A relative
translation
t\in\operatorname{Cone}_{P^{\vee}}Q^{\vee}
gives an embedding

e^{-t}:U_{Q}(\mathfrak{X}_{P})\to\mathfrak{X}_{Q}

(8.34)

defined as
i_{Q}^{{\tilde{P}}}
in (
8.33
) composed with a
translation by
-t
in the
\mathfrak{t}_{Q}
-coordinate in

\mathfrak{X}_{Q}\simeq Z_{Q}\times\mathfrak{t}_{Q}
. Here, we assume

\operatorname{Cone}_{P^{\vee}}Q^{\vee}\subset\mathfrak{t}_{Q}
by fixing a point in

P^{\vee}
to be the origin.

For a sequence of converging broken maps, each with tropical graph

\Gamma
, certain components may escape into cylindrical ends in the
limit, or in the compactified setting, sink into (intersections of)
relative divisors. We examine the convergence of such map
components after applying a translation sequence, which in the
compactified setting, is a sequence of rescalings of the target
space. In order for the translated maps to converge, the
translation sequences must go to infinity (in the sense of
Definition
8.36
below). These translations must be
thought of as happening at a much smaller scale, compared to the
tropical graph
\Gamma
. In other words, for the map component
corresponding to a vertex
v
in
\Gamma
, the tropical position

v_{0}
of the limit of a sequence of translated maps should be seen
as being much closer to
v
, compared to other vertices
v^{\prime}
of

\Gamma
. In fact, one may think of vertex positions in the tropical
graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
of the limit map as

\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}+\epsilon\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{rel}}
, where
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}
is a vertex
position map on
\Gamma
,
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{rel}}
is an element in the
translation sequence for the convergence of the maps, and
\epsilon
is
an infinitesimal.
6
6

6

The last few sentences tell the reader
how to think about translation sequences. These statements can not
be stated formally because, although a tropical graph is
realizable in the dual complex as in Definition

4.6
, the vertex position map is not part of the
data of a tropical map.

However, we avoid the use of infinitesimals in our statements, since
for any
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{rel}}
, there exists
t_{0}>0
such
that for any
t\in(0,t_{0})
,
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}+t\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{rel}}
is a
vertex position map for
\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Definition 8.35
.

(Relative translations)
Suppose

\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
is a tropical edge-collapse morphism,
where
\Gamma
is a tropical graph and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a pre-tropical
graph. For any vertex
v
of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, let

\operatorname{Cone}(\kappa,v):=\operatorname{Cone}_{P(\kappa(v))^{\vee}}(P(v)^{\vee})\\
:=\{\alpha(t-t_{0})\in\mathfrak{t}:t\in P(v)^{\vee},t_{0}\in P(\kappa v)^{\vee},\alpha\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\}

(8.35)

be the cone in the polytope
P(v)^{\vee}
based at points in

P(\kappa(v))^{\vee}
(as in Definition (
3.3
)), and

\operatorname{Cone}(\kappa,v)\subset\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}
by fixing a point in

P(\kappa(v))^{\vee}
to be the origin. A
relative translation

or a
(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translation
is an element

\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma}=(\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma}(v)\in\operatorname{Cone}(\kappa,v))_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})}

satisfying

\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma}(v_{+})-\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma}(v_{-})\in\begin{cases}\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\mathcal{T}(e),&e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma^{\prime})\backslash\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma),\\
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{T}(e),&e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma)\end{cases}

(8.36)

for any edge
e=(v_{+},v_{-})
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
. The space of
(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translations is denoted by
w(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
.

Relative translations appearing in the convergence of broken maps

go to infinity
in the following sense:

Definition 8.36
.

(Relative translation sequence going to infinity)

Suppose

\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
is a tropical edge collapse,
where
\Gamma
is a tropical graph and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a pre-tropical graph.
A

(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translation sequence

t_{\nu}(v)\in\operatorname{Cone}(\kappa,v)=\operatorname{Cone}_{P(\kappa v)^{\vee}}(P(v)^{\vee}),\quad v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})

(where
\operatorname{Cone}(\kappa,v)
is as
defined in (
8.35
))
goes to infinity
if

(a)

(Polytope) For any vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and a
polytope
Q\in\mathcal{P}
such that

P(v)\subset Q\subseteq P(\kappa(v))
,

d(t_{\nu}(v),\operatorname{Cone}_{P(\kappa v)^{\vee}}(Q^{\vee}))\to\infty.

(b)

(Direction for collapsed edge) For any edge

e\in\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma_{\kappa^{-1}(v)})
connecting vertices
v_{+}
,

v_{-}
, the sequence
l_{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}
defined as

t_{\nu}(v_{+})-t_{\nu}(v_{-})=\mathcal{T}(e)l_{\nu}

goes to infinity, that is,
l_{\nu}\to\infty
.
(Note that the existence of
l_{\nu}
follows from the Direction condition (
8.36
) on relative translations.)

The following result says that the existence of a

(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translation sequence implies that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a
tropical graph, in that it has a vertex position map. The proof is
straightforward and is left to the reader.

Lemma 8.37
.

Suppose
\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
is a tropical edge-collapse
morphism, where
\Gamma
is a tropical graph and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a
pre-tropical graph. Suppose
\{\mathcal{T}_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
is a sequence of

(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translations going to infinity. Then,
\Gamma^{\prime}

is a tropical graph. For any vertex position
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}
of

\Gamma
and a large enough
\nu
, there is a constant
t_{0}>0
such
that

\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})\ni v\mapsto\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}(\kappa(v))+t\mathcal{T}_{\nu}(v)

is a vertex position for
\Gamma^{\prime}
for all
t\in(0,t_{0})
.

Remark 8.38
.

(The space of relative translations is a cone) Given a tropical
edge collapse morphism
\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
between
tropical graphs
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Gamma
, the space of relative
translations
w(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
is a cone

w(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)=\operatorname{Cone}_{\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)}\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma^{\prime})

(8.37)

based at the polytope
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
(as in Definition

3.3
). Here, we recall from Remark
4.8

that the closures of the spaces of vertex positions

\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)
are polytopes, and

\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma)\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{W}}(\Gamma^{\prime}),\quad\mathcal{T}\mapsto(v\mapsto\mathcal{T}(\kappa(v))

is a face. We obtain (
8.37
) by observing that for
any vertex position maps
\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{W}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and

\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{W}(\Gamma)
, and
\alpha\geq 0
, the scaled
difference

\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma^{\prime})\ni v\mapsto\alpha(\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(v)-\mathcal{T}_{\Gamma}(\kappa v))\in\mathfrak{t}_{P(v)}

is a
(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
-translation. If
\Gamma
is rigid,

\mathcal{W}(\Gamma)
is a single point, and the space
w(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)
of
relative translations is a cone based at a single point.

Example 8.39
.

For the tropical edge
collapse
\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
in both Figure

4.5
and Figure
8.1
, the tropical graph

\Gamma
is rigid, and the space of vertex positions of
\Gamma^{\prime}
is
(linearly) isomorphic to the interval
(0,1)
, where
0
corresponds
to the vertex position where the edges collapsed by
\kappa
have
length
0
, and therefore, by (
4.11
), is not a vertex
position for
\Gamma^{\prime}
; and
1
corresponds to some vertex
v
of

\Gamma^{\prime}
lying in a lower dimensional polytope

Q^{\vee}\subset P^{\vee}(v)
, and so, is not a legitimate vertex
position of
\Gamma^{\prime}
. In both cases the space of relative
translations is

w(\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma)=\operatorname{Cone}_{0}[0,1]=\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}.

8.5.2
Defining Gromov convergence of broken maps

The definition of Gromov convergence for broken maps is largely similar to the definition of convergence of breaking maps (Definition
8.13
) with the following two differences:

—

The definition of convergence for breaking maps is given for a
sequence of irreducible maps in neck-stretched manifolds. For
convergence of broken maps, we need to consider maps with multiple
components. Thus, in the definition below, we give the conditions
under which a sequence of broken maps with tropical graph
\Gamma

converges to a broken map with tropical graph
\Gamma^{\prime}
, where the
tropical graphs are related by an edge collapse

\kappa:\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
. For each vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)

of the tropical graph, the sequence of smooth maps
u_{v,\nu}
, with
markings corresponding to nodal lifts
w_{e}\in C_{v}
, converges to a
limit map
u_{v}
. The limit map
u_{v}
may have multiple components,
corresponding to vertices that are collapsed to
v
by the morphism

\kappa
. That is,
u_{v}=u|(\cup_{v^{\prime}\in\kappa^{-1}(v)}C_{v^{\prime}})
.

—

Translations occurring in the convergence of breaking maps are
replaced by relative translations for the case of convergence of
broken maps. The corresponding maps of manifolds
{\mathbb{e}}^{-t}
in the
breaking case (see Definition
3.65
) are replaced by

e^{-t}
(see (
8.34
)) in the case of broken maps.

Definition 8.40
.

(Gromov convergence for broken maps) Suppose
\Gamma^{\prime}
,

\Gamma
are combinatorial types of stable treed disks that are
equipped with a tropical structure. Suppose

\Gamma^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
is a tropical edge collapse
morphism which induces a vertex map
