[d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})]:=\{1,\dots,d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})\}
. In the first union,
j
is
the index of the attaching leaf in the quilted tree and so ranges from

1
to
n-i+1
. The first union ranges over all partitions of the
set
[d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})]
into
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
. The second union ranges over
partitions of
[d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})]
into
I_{0},\dots,I_{r}
. By construction,
for the facet of the first type, the sign of the inclusions of
boundary strata are the same as that for the corresponding inclusion
of boundary facets of the moduli space

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}
of unquilted disks, that is,

(-1)^{i(n-i-j)+j}
. For facets of the second type, the gluing map
is

(0,\infty)\times\mathcal{M}_{r,m_{0}}\times\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathcal{M}^{q}_{|I_{j}|,m_{j}}\to\mathcal{M}^{q}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}

given for boundary markings by

(\delta,x_{1},\ldots,x_{r},(x_{1,j}=0,x_{2,j},\ldots,x_{m_{j},j})_{j=1}^{r})\mapsto\\
(x_{1},x_{1}+\delta^{-1}x_{2,1},\ldots,x_{1}+\delta^{-1}x_{m_{1},1},\ldots,x_{r},x_{r}+\delta^{-1}x_{2,r},\ldots,x_{r}+\delta^{-1}x_{m_{r},r}).

(10.34)

This map views the markings as lying in the affine half plane

\mathbb{H}\subset\mathbb{C}
; an interior
x\in I_{j}
is mapped to

x_{j}+\delta^{-1}x
; and the map is well-defined for
\delta
that is
large enough to ensure that the ordering of the boundary markings is
preserved. This map changes orientations by

{\textstyle\sum}_{j=1}^{r}(r-j)(m_{j}-1);
in case of non-trivial weightings,

m_{j}
should be replaced by the number of incoming markings
plus non-trivial but finite weightings on the
j
-th component.

10.5
Quilted pseudoholomorphic disks

In this Section and the next, we prove that the Fukaya algebra of a
Lagrangian submanifold is independent of the choice of perturbation
data up to homotopy equivalence. Given
A_{\infty}
algebras defined using
perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
whose underlying
stabilizing divisor has the same degree, in this Section, we construct
an
A_{\infty}
morphism between them. The
A_{\infty}
morphism is defined by
counts of quilted treed holomorphic disks. The perturbation system
corresponding to such disks is
called a
perturbation morphism
, and is
defined by extending the perturbation
systems
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
; that is, the perturbation system for
quilted holomorphic disks restricts to
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
on
light and dark unquilted components respectively.

The following is the main result
which shows
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence for
A_{\infty}
algebras defined using two different perturbations whose stabilizing divisors have the same degree. We refer the reader to
[
17
, Section 5.6]
for the case when the stabilizing divisors have different degrees.
In the statement of the result,
a unital
A_{\infty}
morphism is defined in (
10.9
),
a convergent
A_{\infty}
morphism is defined in Remark
10.19
, and perturbation morphisms
are from Definition
10.20
.

Proposition 10.18
.

Suppose
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
are regular perturbation data that are
defined using stabilizing pairs
(J^{0},D^{0})
and
(J^{1},D^{1})
, which
are connected by a path of stabilizing pairs
\{(J^{t},D^{t})\}_{t\in[0,1]}
. There exists a coherent perturbation
morphism

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}

which induces a convergent unital
A_{\infty}
morphism

\phi:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}),

which is a convergent unital
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence.

Proposition
10.18
is used in Section

10.7
to show that the Fukaya algebra of a Lagrangian in
a neck-stretched manifold is independent of the neck length parameter
up to homotopy equivalence. The
A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi

required by Proposition
10.18
is
constructed later in this Section after defining quilted holomorphic
disks. The fact that
\phi
is a homotopy equivalence is proved
in Section
10.6
as part of Corollary
10.30
.

Remark 10.19
.

(Convergent
A_{\infty}
morphisms) An
A_{\infty}
morphism

\mathcal{F}:A_{0}\to A_{1}
between
A_{\infty}
algebras with Novikov coefficients
is said to be
convergent
if

\mathcal{F}^{0}
has positive
q
-valuation. A convergent
A_{\infty}
morphism

\mathcal{F}:A_{0}\to A_{1}
, induces a well-defined map

MC(\mathcal{F}):MC(A_{0})\to MC(A_{1})
on the space of Maurer-Cartan
solutions and a map on cohomology

H(\mathcal{F}):H(A_{0},b)\to H(A_{1},\mathcal{F}(b))
for any
b\in MC(A_{0})
,
see
[
17
, Lemma 5.2, 5.3]
. In Proposition

10.18
, the
A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi
being convergent
means that
\phi^{0}(1)\in\Lambda_{>0}\langle{\hat{\mathcal{I}}(L)}\rangle
.

Definition 10.20
.

Given perturbation data

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}=(J^{0}_{\Gamma},F^{0}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma},\quad\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}=(J^{1}_{\Gamma},F^{1}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}

on unquilted
treed disks with respect to stabilizing divisors
D^{0}
and
D^{1}

that have the same degree and a path
\{D^{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
of Donaldson divisors,
a
perturbation morphism

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
from
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
to
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
for the type
\Gamma
of a quilted treed disk consists of

(a)

a domain-dependent Morse function

F_{\Gamma}^{01}:\overline{\mathcal{T}}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},\Gamma}\to\mathbb{R},

which for
k=0,1
, is equal to the domain-independent Morse
function
F^{k}
on the neighbourhood

\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},\Gamma}-\overline{{\mathcal{T}}}^{\operatorname{cp}}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},\Gamma}

of the endpoints for which the distance to the quilting circle

\delta\circ d
is
k
, where
F^{k}:L\to\mathbb{R}
is the background
Morse function for
F^{k}_{\Gamma}
for
k=0,1
;

(b)

and a domain-dependent almost complex structure

J_{\Gamma}^{01}:\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\Gamma}\to\bigsqcup_{t\in[0,1]}\{J\in\mathcal{J}(X,D^{t}):J\text{ is $\omega$-tamed}\},

with the property that

—

on the curve
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma,m}\subset\overline{\mathcal{S}}_{\Gamma}

associated to any point
m\in\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
,
J_{\Gamma}^{01}
is
locally constant on
\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma,m}-\mathcal{S}^{\operatorname{cp}}_{\Gamma,m}
,
where the compact set
\mathcal{S}^{\operatorname{cp}}
is as defined in Section

6.1
; and on a component

\mathcal{S}_{v,m}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma,m}
corresponding to

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
,
J_{\Gamma}^{01}
is adapted to the divisor

D^{\delta\circ d(v)}
where
d
is the distance from the quilting circle
function (
10.31
), that is,

J_{\Gamma}^{01}(\mathcal{S}_{v,m})\subset\mathcal{J}(X,D^{\delta\circ d(v)})
.

—

Furthermore, for
k=0,1
, denoting by
\Gamma_{k}\subset\Gamma

the sub-tree where the distance from the quilting circle
\delta\circ d

is
k
,
J_{\Gamma}^{01}
is equal to the domain-dependent complex
structure
J_{\Gamma_{k}}^{k}
on the (unquilted) treed disk
component of type
\Gamma_{k}
.

A collection of perturbation morphisms
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}=(\mathfrak{p}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}

defined on moduli spaces of quilted disks is
coherent
if it is
compatible with the (Cutting edges), (Making an edge length/weight
finite or non-zero) and (Forgetting
edges) morphisms on weighted quilted disk types as in Remark
10.17
,
and satisfies the (Locality axiom) from Definition
6.4
.

Next, we define the quilted version of perturbed holomorphic treed disks (Definition

6.11
).

Definition 10.21
.

(Holomorphic quilted treed disk)

Let

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}=(\mathfrak{p}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}
be a coherent perturbation morphism.
A holomorphic quilted treed disk is a map
u:C\to X
where
C
is
a quilted disk of type
\Gamma
, and
u
is
\mathfrak{p}_{\Gamma}
-holomorphic
in the sense of ordinary perturbed holomorphic disks (see Definition

6.11
), and
adapted
to a family of divisors

\{D^{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
in the sense that

—

each
interior marking
z_{i}
maps to

D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i})}
, where
d
is the distance from the quilting circle
(
10.31
) and
\delta
is from
(
10.32
),

—

and for
t\in[0,1]
, each component of

u^{-1}(D^{t})\cap(\delta\circ d)^{-1}(t)
contains a marking.

If the domain quilted treed disk
C
is unstable, we obtain a stable
quilted treed disk
C^{\prime}
by collapsing unstable surface and tree
components, and we denote the collapsing map by
f:C\to C^{\prime}
.
Pulling back by
f
we obtain a datum on
C
, still denoted

(J^{01}_{\Gamma},F^{01}_{\Gamma})
.
A quilted holomorphic treed disk
u:C\to X
is
stable
if
every (surface or tree) component of
C
on which
u
is
non-constant is stable in the sense of weighted quilted disks. The

type
of a quilted holomorphic disk consists of the
combinatorial type of the domain quilted treed disk, and the
tangency and homology data of the map components as in the type of a
treed holomorphic disk, see Definition
6.13
.

Remark 10.22
.

(Symplectic area and the number of markings)
For a quilted treed disk
C=S\cup T
, on any surface component

S_{v}\subset S
, the function
\delta\circ d|S_{v}
is constant and
therefore a quilted holomorphic disk
u:C\to X
is adapted to the
divisor
D^{\delta\circ d(S_{v})}
on the component
S_{v}
.
Furthermore, the divisor
D^{\delta\circ d(S_{v})}
is holomorphic
with respect to the background almost complex structure

J^{\delta\circ d(S_{v})}
, and therefore all intersections between
the map
u|S_{v}
and the divisor
D^{\delta\circ d(S_{v})}
are
positive. The intersection number of
u
with
D^{\delta}
is

k\omega[u]
, where the divisor
D_{0}
(and
D_{1}
) is Poincaré dual
to
k\omega
.

Remark 10.23
.

(Quilted holomorphic disks for a path of perturbations)

(a)

Often a perturbation morphism is constructed using a generic
path of perturbations
\mathfrak{p}^{t}_{\Gamma}=(J^{t}_{\Gamma},F_{\Gamma})
,

t\in[0,1]
, whose Morse datum
F_{\Gamma}
is
t
-independent.
Let
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
be a quilted disk type, for which forgetting the
quilting yields the disk type
\Gamma
. One may define a
perturbation morphism
\mathfrak{p}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}^{01}
connecting
\mathfrak{p}^{0}_{\Gamma}

and
\mathfrak{p}^{1}_{\Gamma}
by setting the domain-dependent data to be

J_{\tilde{\Gamma}}^{01}(z):=J^{\delta\circ d(z)}_{\Gamma}(z),\quad F_{\tilde{\Gamma}}^{01}(z):=F_{\Gamma}(z)

(10.35)

for any
z\in\mathcal{S}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}
where
d
is the “distance from the quilting circle’
function from (
10.31
), and

\delta:[-\infty,\infty]\to[0,1]
is a fixed increasing
diffeomorphism from (
10.32
). Thus on any surface
component of a
J_{\tilde{\Gamma}}^{01}
-holomorphic quilted disk, the map is

J^{t}_{\Gamma}
-holomorphic for some
t\in[0,1]
.

(b)

(A path of regular perturbations) In
some special cases, a one-dimensional component of quilted disks is
made up of a family of unquilted
\mathfrak{p}_{t}
-holomorphic disks for

t\in[0,1]
. Such a special case arises when the perturbation

\mathfrak{p}^{t}_{\Gamma}=(J^{t}_{\Gamma},F_{\Gamma})
is regular for a disk homology
class
\beta\in H_{2}(X,L)
and input/output tuple

\underline{x}=(x_{0},\dots,x_{d})
for all
t
. Then the zero dimensional
component of the moduli space of perturbed holomorphic maps

\mathcal{M}_{\beta}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{t},\underline{x})_{0}
is in bijection with

\mathcal{M}_{\beta}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0},\underline{x})_{0}
for any
t\in[0,1]
. Furthermore,
for a perturbation morphism as in (
10.35
), a path of
moduli spaces

\cup_{t\in[0,1]}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{t},\underline{x})^{\leq E_{0}}_{0}
of

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{t}
-holomorphic disks gives a one-dimensional component

(u_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
of quilted
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
-holomorphic disks
where

—

if
\delta^{-1}(t)>0
(where

\delta:[-\infty,\infty]\to[0,1]
is a fixed increasing
diffeomorphism),
u_{t}
is of the form in Figure
10.9

(a),
u_{t}
is constant on quilted components,
J_{t}
-holomorphic on
the dark shaded disk, and boundary edges have length

\tau:=\delta^{-1}(t)
;

—

if
\delta^{-1}(t)<0
,
u_{t}
is of the form in Figure

10.9
(c),
u_{t}
is constant on quilted components and

J_{t}
-holomorphic on the light shaded disk, and boundary edges
have length
\tau:=-\delta^{-1}(t)
;

—

if
\delta^{-1}(t)=0
,
u_{t}
is of the form in Figure

10.9
(b), on the quilted components
u_{t}
is

J_{t}
-holomorphic.

Abbildung 10.9:
Types of quilted holomorphic disks occurring in the one-dimensional moduli space described in Remark
10.23
(
b
).
In (a) and (c) the map is constant on the quilted component.

The analysis of the special case shows that in an idealized setting
moduli spaces of quilted holomorphic disks interpolate between moduli
spaces of unquilted holomorphic disks. But in general cases, one has
to account for disk bubbling in the moduli spaces, leading to a
variety of configurations occurring in the codimension one boundary of
moduli spaces of quilted holomorphic disks, see Remark

10.25
.

For any combinatorial type
\Gamma
of quilted holomorphic disks we
denote by
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{q}_{\Gamma}(X,L)
the compactified moduli
space of equivalence classes of treed quilted holomorphic
disks. The moduli space of quilted disks breaks into components
depending on the limits along the root and leaf edges. Denote by

\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}^{q}(X,L,\underline{x})\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{q}(X,L)

the moduli space of isomorphism classes of stable
holomorphic
quilted treed disks of type
\Gamma
with boundary in
L
and limits

\underline{x}
along the root and leaf edges, where

\underline{x}=(x_{0},\ldots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})\in\widehat{\mathcal{I}}(L)
.
A quilted holomorphic disk type
\Gamma
is
rigid
if
it satisfies all the conditions for a holomorphic map type to be rigid, as in Definition

8.48
.
For a rigid quilted holomorphic disk type
\Gamma
, the expected dimension of the moduli space

\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}^{q}(X,L,\underline{x})
is

i^{q}(\Gamma,\underline{x})=i(\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x})+1,

(10.36)

where
i
is the index for types of treed holomorphic disks (see
(
6.31
)), and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is the unquilted type obtained by
forgetting the quilting in
\Gamma
. The extra dimension for the
moduli space of quilted disks arises from the quilting datum. In case

n
quilted disk components, there are
n-1
equations in the

(Equal lengths condition)
, leading to an extra
dimension of
1
compared to the unquilted case.

For a comeager subset of perturbation morphisms extending those chosen
for unquilted disks, the uncrowded moduli spaces of expected dimension
at most one are smooth and of expected dimension. For sequential
compactness, it suffices to consider a sequence
u_{\nu}:C_{\nu}\to X

of quilted treed disks of fixed combinatorial type
\Gamma=\Gamma_{\nu}

for all
\nu
. Coherence of the perturbation morphism implies the
existence of a stable limit
u:C\to X
which we claim is adapted.
If a sequence of markings
z_{i,\nu}\in C_{\nu}
converges to

z_{i}\in C
, then,
u(z_{i})\in D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i})}
. Indeed, since
the distance from the quilting circle
d(z_{i,\nu})
converges to
d(z_{i})
, the
divisor
D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i})}
is the limit of the divisors

D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i,\nu})}
. For types of index at most one, each
component of
u^{-1}(D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i})})
is a limit of a unique
component of
u^{-1}_{\nu}(D^{\delta\circ d(z_{i,\nu})})
, otherwise
the intersection degree would be more than one which is a codimension
two condition. Therefore, every marking in
C
is a transverse divisor
intersection. There are no other divisor intersections because the
intersection number with
D^{\delta\circ d}
is preserved in the
limit for topological reasons. The moduli space of quilted broken disks then has the same
transversality and compactness property as in the unquilted case, by
similar arguments.

Counts of quilted disks define
A_{\infty}
morphisms.
Given a regular, stabilized and coherent perturbation morphism

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
from
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
to
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
, define an
A_{\infty}
morphism

\phi=(\phi^{d})_{d\geq 0}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1})
as

\phi^{d}:CF(L;\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})^{\otimes d}\to CF(L;\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1})\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\mapsto\sum_{x_{0},u\in\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}(L,D,x_{0},\ldots,x_{d})_{0}}(-1)^{\heartsuit}w(u)x_{0}

(10.37)

where the weight
w(u)
is given by

w(u)=\epsilon([u])(d_{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}(\Gamma)!)^{-1}q^{E([u])}\operatorname{Hol}_{L}([\partial u])x_{0}

(10.38)

the sum is over uncrowded strata
\Gamma
of weighted treed quilted
holomorphic disks whose boundary edges

e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)
have finite non-zero length

\ell(e)\in(0,\infty)
and whose input and output labels are
compatible with
(x_{0},\dots,x_{d})
in terms of the (Label axiom) in
Definition
10.8
, and
\epsilon([u])=\pm 1
is the
orientation sign.

Remark 10.24
.

(Lowest area terms) For any

x\in{\operatorname{crit}}(F^{0})\cup\{x^{\includegraphics[width]{greyt.pdf}},x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}\}
, the element

\phi_{1}(x)
contains zero area terms coming from the count of a
quilted treed disk with no interior marking, that is, a treed disk
with only one disk that is quilted and mapped to a point. The domain
is one of those in Figure
10.8
. If
x
is
x^{\includegraphics[width]{greyt.pdf}}

resp.
x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}
there is one such configuration whose output is
weighted resp. forgettable. In the latter case, it will be the only
term with a forgettable output.

Remark 10.25
.

(Codimension one boundary
strata) The codimension one strata are of several possible types:
either there is one (or a collection of) edge(s)
e
of length

\ell(e)
infinity, there is one (or a collection of) edge(s)
e
of
length
\ell(e)
zero, or equivalently, boundary nodes, or there is
an edge
e
with zero or infinite weight
\rho(e)
. The case of an
edge of zero or infinite weighting is equivalent to breaking off a
constant trajectory, and so may be ignored. In the case of edges of
infinite length(s), then either
\Gamma
is

(a)

(Breaking off an unquilted tree) a pair

\Gamma_{1}\sqcup\Gamma_{2}
consisting of a quilted tree
\Gamma_{1}

and an unquilted tree
\Gamma_{2}
as in the left side of Figure

10.6
; necessarily the breaking must be a leaf of

\Gamma_{1}
; or

(b)

(Breaking off quilted trees) a collection consisting of
an unquilted tree
\Gamma_{0}
containing the root and a collection

\Gamma_{1},\ldots,\Gamma_{r}
of quilted trees attached to each of
its
r
leaves as in the right side of Figure
10.6
.
Such a stratum
\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}
is codimension one because of the

(Equal lengths condition)

which implies
that if the length of any edge between
e_{0}
to
e_{i}
is infinite
for some
i
then the path from
e_{0}
to
e_{i}
for any
i
has the
same property.

In the case of a zero length(s), one obtains a fake boundary
component with normal bundle
\mathbb{R}
, corresponding to either deforming
the edge(s) to have non-zero length or deforming the node(s). This
ends the Remark.

We can now prove the first part of Proposition
10.18
.

Proof of Proposition
10.18
.

We have so far chosen a regular coherent perturbation morphism
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
, and defined
a collection
\phi=(\phi^{d})_{d\geq 0}

of maps
in (
10.37
), which is a convergent unital
A_{\infty}
morphism for the following reason:
The true boundary strata of one-dimensional moduli spaces of quilted
holomorphic disks are those described in Remark
10.25
and
correspond to the terms in the axiom for
A_{\infty}
morphisms
(
10.8
).
The signs are similar to those in
[
17
]
and
omitted. The assertion on the strict units is a consequence of the
existence of forgetful maps for infinite values of the weights. By
assumption the terms involving
\phi_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}
and

x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}
as inputs involve counts of quilted treed disks using
perturbation that are independent of the disk boundary incidence
points of the first leaf marked
x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}
asymptotic to

x_{M}\in X
. Since forgetting that semi-infinite edge gives a
configuration of negative expected dimension, if non-constant, the
only configurations contributing to these terms must be the constant
maps. Hence

\phi^{1}(x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}})=x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}},\quad\phi^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}(\ldots,x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}},\dots)=0,n\geq 2.

In other words, the only regular quilted trajectories from the
maximum, considered as
x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}
, being regular are the ones
reaching the other maximum that do not have interior markings
(i.e. non-constant disks). The convergence property follows from the
fact that any treed quilted disk with no incoming edges must, by
stability, have a disk component with interior markings. The
A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi
is an
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence by Corollary

10.30
.
∎

10.6
Homotopies

In this Section, we define homotopies between
A_{\infty}
morphisms via
counts of twice-quilted disks. The main result is that the
A_{\infty}
morphism between Fukaya algebras constructed in the previous Section
is a homotopy equivalence.

A
twice-quilted disk

(C,Q_{1},Q_{2})
is defined
in the same way as once-quilted disks, but with two quilting circles

Q_{1},Q_{2}\subset C
that are either equal
Q_{1}=Q_{2}
or with the
second
Q_{2}\subset\operatorname{int}(Q_{1})
contained inside the first, say
with radii
\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}
satisfying certain balancing conditions.

The combinatorial type
\Gamma
of a nodal
quilted disk comes with the data of subsets of the boundary vertex set

\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{1}(\Gamma),\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma)\subset\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma),\quad\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma)=\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{1}(\Gamma)\cap\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma),

where
\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{k}(\Gamma)
corresponds to disk components which contain the quilting circle
Q_{i}
.

The vertices in
\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma)
are
twice-quilted vertices
.

See Figure
10.10
.
For a twice-quilted vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma)
, we denote by

\lambda_{S}(v):=\rho_{2}(v)/\rho_{1}(v)

(10.39)

the ratio
of the radii of the quilting circles.

Abbildung 10.10:
For
k=1,2
, components corresponding to vertices in
\operatorname{Vert}_{k}^{q}\subset\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}
contain the quilting circle
Q_{i}
. Components corresponding to vertices
in
\operatorname{Vert}_{12}^{qq}\subset\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}
are twice-quilted.

Treed twice-quilted disks are defined by replacing disk nodes
by treed segments, whose lengths satisfy two equal lengths conditions:
The lengths of edges occurring in a path
P
from the root vertex to
a quilted disk vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}_{2}^{q}(\Gamma)
containing the inner quilting circle
Q_{2}
is independent of
v
:
For any two vertices
v_{1},v_{2}\in\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma)
connected to the root vertex
v_{0}
by non self-crossing paths
P(v_{0},v_{1})
,
P(v_{0},v_{2})
in
\Gamma
,

{\text{{(Equal lengths to inner circle)}}}\quad\sum_{e\in P(v_{0},v_{1})}\ell(e)=\sum_{e\in P(v_{0},v_{2})}\ell(e).

(10.40)

Secondly, for a fixed disk vertex
v_{2}\in\operatorname{Vert}_{2}^{q}(\Gamma)
containing an inner quilting circle,
and any two vertices
v_{1},v_{1}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Vert}_{1}^{q}(\Gamma)
containing an outer quilting circle and for which the paths
P(v_{1},v_{2})
,
P(v_{1}^{\prime},v_{2})
do not containing the root vertex (unless
v_{2}
is the root vertex),

{\text{{(Equal lengths from inner to outer circle)}}}\quad\sum_{e\in P(v_{2},v_{1})}\ell(e)=\sum_{e\in P(v_{2},v_{1}^{\prime})}\ell(e).

(10.41)

Note that if
v_{2}
is a twice-quilted vertex, then (
10.41
) is automatically satisfied since all path lengths occurring in (
10.41
) are zero.
A twice-quilted disk is
stable
if it has at least two special points.

The treed disks
with two quilting circles defined so far are called
unbalanced twice-quilted disks
. The moduli space of unbalanced treed twice quilted disks of type
\Gamma
is denoted by
M^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}
.
An unbalanced twice-quilted disk
C\in\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}

is a
twice-quilted disk

if it satisfies the
balanced ratio of radii
condition, which is as follows:

—

Either all quilted disks are twice-quilted, that is
\operatorname{Vert}_{1}^{q}(\Gamma)=\operatorname{Vert}_{2}^{q}(\Gamma)=\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma)
, and the
ratio (
10.39
) of radii
is required to be the same for all twice-quilted vertices

v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma)
, that is,

{\text{{(Balanced ratios, surfaces)}}}\quad\lambda_{S}(v)=\lambda_{S}(v^{\prime})\quad\forall v,v^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Vert}^{qq}_{12}(\Gamma);

(10.42)

or

—

there are no twice-quilted disks and the following treed version of the balanced ratio condition holds: For a disk vertex

v_{2}\in\operatorname{Vert}^{2}(\Gamma)
containing the inner circle, and a vertex

v_{1}\in\operatorname{Vert}^{1}(\Gamma)
containing the outer quilting circle, and
whose path to the root vertex contains
v_{2}
, denoting

\lambda_{T}(v_{2}):=\sum_{e\in P(v_{1},v_{2})}\ell(e),

(10.43)

where
P(v_{1},v_{2})
is the path in
\Gamma
from
v_{1}
to
v_{2}
,
we require that

(Balanced ratios, trees)

\lambda_{T}
is independent of
v_{2}
.

(10.44)

Note that by (
10.41
), the quantity
\lambda_{T}(v_{2})
does
not depend on the choice of
v_{1}
. The balanced ratio conditions
(
10.42
), (
10.44
) on surfaces and trees can be stated
in a unified way as follows. First, we fix an identification

\phi:((\{1\leq\lambda_{S}\leq\infty\}\sqcup\{0\leq\lambda_{T}\leq\infty\})/(\lambda_{S}=\infty)\sim(\lambda_{T}=0))\xrightarrow{\simeq}[0,\infty].

Define a
generalized ratio of radii
as

\lambda_{\Gamma}:\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}\to[0,\infty]^{\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma)},\quad\lambda_{\Gamma}(C)=(\lambda_{\Gamma}(C)_{v})_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma)},

where, for any vertex
v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{2}(\Gamma)
containing an
inner quilting circle,

\lambda_{\Gamma}(C)_{v}:=\phi\left(\begin{cases}\begin{cases}\lambda_{S}(v),\quad\text{$v$ is twice-quilted},\\
\lambda_{T}(v).\quad\text{$v$ is not twice-quilted}\end{cases}\end{cases}\right),

An unbalanced twice-quilted curve
C
of type
\Gamma
then satisfies the balanced ratio condition if
\lambda_{\Gamma}(C)
maps to the diagonal
\Delta
, that is,
\lambda_{\Gamma}(C)_{v}
is the same for all
v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{q}_{1}(\Gamma)
. The moduli space of twice quilted disks of type
\Gamma
is denoted by

\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma}^{qq}:=\lambda_{\Gamma}^{-1}(\Delta).

The moduli space of twice-quilted treed
disks is a cell complex constructed in a similar way to the space of
once-quilted treed disks.
Denote the moduli space
of twice-quilted disks
with
d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
boundary leaves and
d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})
interior leaves by
\mathcal{M}^{qq}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}
.
The moduli space admits a
stratification by combinatorial type, that is,

\mathcal{M}^{qq}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}=\cup_{\Gamma}\mathcal{M}^{qq}_{\Gamma},

where
\Gamma
ranges over all twice-quilted disk types with

d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
non-root boundary leaves and
d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})
interior leaves. The open strata in

\mathcal{M}^{qq}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}
have dimension

d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})+2d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}).

A twice-quilted holomorphic disk type
\Gamma
is
rigid

if it satisfies all the conditions for a holomorphic map type to be rigid, as in Definition

8.48
.

For example, the moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{qq}_{1,0}
of twice-quilted disks
with a single non-root boundary marking is one-dimensional. It has two
open strata interspersed by three strata of codimension one, whose
types are shown in Figure
10.11
.

Abbildung 10.11:
Moduli space of treed twice-quilted disks with one leaf

Abbildung 10.12:
Moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{qq}
of twice-quilted disks with two boundary inputs. The figure shows the configurations at the codimension two corners, and also the end-points of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{0,2}^{{qq},\lambda_{S}}
with fixed ratio of radii.

Coherent domain-dependent perturbations for twice-quilted disks are defined in
a similar way to quilted disks, see
[
17
, Definition
5.9]
. That is, given coherent perturbations
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
,

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
on
X
, and coherent perturbation morphisms

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{01}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{12}
, perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}
for
twice quilted disks are required to interpolate between these. In
particular, if in a stratum, (Cutting edges) produces an unquilted disk
type
\Gamma
of shading
k
resp. a once-quilted disk type
\Gamma

of shading
k
and
k+1
, then the domain-dependent perturbation on
these strata is defined by the pullback of
(\mathfrak{p}_{k})_{\Gamma}

resp.
(\mathfrak{p}_{k,k+1})_{\Gamma}
.

The following is the main result of the Section.

Theorem 10.26
.

(
A_{\infty}
homotopies via
twice-quilted disks) Given coherent perturbations

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{01},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{12},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{02}
defining morphisms

\phi_{ij}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{i})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{j}),\quad 0\leq i<j\leq 2

and coherent perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}
for
twice-quilted disks, there is a convergent
A_{\infty}
homotopy

\mathcal{T}^{02}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\phi_{02},\phi_{01}\circ\phi_{12}).

Remark 10.27
.

(Convergent
A_{\infty}
homotopies) Let
A_{0}
,
A_{1}
be
A_{\infty}
algebras with Novikov coefficients, and let

\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{1}:A_{0}\to A_{1}
be
A_{\infty}
morphisms An
A_{\infty}
homotopy

\mathcal{T}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{1})
from
\mathcal{F}_{0}
to
\mathcal{F}_{1}
is said to be

convergent
if
\mathcal{T}^{0}
has
positive
q
-valuation. For a convergent
A_{\infty}
algebra
A
, if a
convergent morphism
\mathcal{F}:A\to A
is convergent homotopy equivalent
to the identity morphism, then the map on the space of Maurer-Cartan
solutions
MC(\mathcal{F}):MC(A)\to MC(A)
, and the map on cohomology

H(\mathcal{F}):H(A,b)\to H(A,\mathcal{F}(b))
for any
b\in MC(A)
are
isomorphisms modulo gauge transformations, see
[
17
, Lemma 5.2,
5.3]
.

Proof of Theorem
10.26
.

We first introduce the necessary moduli spaces of holomorphic twice-quilted
disks.
For a generic perturbation
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}
that extends perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}
,
i=0,1,2
, and perturbation morphisms

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{01}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{12}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{02}
, and
\lambda
lying in a comeager subset
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{reg}\subset[0,\infty]
, for any type
\Gamma
of twice-quilted disks, and any collection
\underline{x}
of labels on the leaves,

—

the moduli
space
\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda}_{\Gamma}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012},\underline{x})_{d}
of twice quilted
disks of type
\Gamma
with ratio of radii is
\lambda
and expected dimension
d\leq 1
, and

—

the moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{{qq}}_{\Gamma}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012},\underline{x})_{d}
of twice
quilted disks with the ratio of radii unprescribed with expected dimension
d\leq 0

are transversally cut out.
For any
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{reg}
as above,
counts of rigid twice-quilted holomorphic disks with ratio of
radii
\lambda
and index zero defines an

A_{\infty}
morphism

\phi_{02}^{\lambda}=((\phi_{02}^{\lambda})^{d})_{d},\qquad(\phi_{02}^{\lambda})^{d}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})^{\otimes d}\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}).

For
\lambda=0
,
the
A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi_{02}^{0}
corresponds to
\phi_{02}
, and for
\lambda=\infty
,

\phi_{02}^{\infty}
corresponds to
\phi_{12}\circ\phi_{01}
.

The homotopy between
\phi_{02}^{0}
and
\phi_{02}^{\infty}
is defined by combining homotopies constructed from
small variations of ratio. We aim to define a pre-natural
transformation

\mathcal{T}^{02}=((\mathcal{T}^{02})^{d})_{d\geq 0}\in\operatorname{Hom}(\phi_{02}^{0},\phi_{02}^{\infty})
which will later be shown to be a homotopy.
Given an energy bound
E>0
, we first define
\mathcal{T}^{02}
modulo terms involving
q^{\geq E}
.
We may divide
[0,\infty]
into a finite number of intervals

[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}],\quad i=1,\dots,k,

(10.45)

so that there is at
most one rigid twice-quilted disk with ratio
\lambda
and
symplectic area
\leq E
(which, we recall from (
5.2
), is
proportional to the number of interior markings
d_{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}
) in each
such interval
[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]
. We define
\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1},\leq E}

by counting twice-quilted disks with ratio in the specified
interval,

(\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1},\leq E})^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})^{\otimes d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{2})\\
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})\mapsto\sum_{x_{0},u\in\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda}_{\Gamma}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}},\underline{x})_{0}}w(u)x_{0},

(10.46)

where
\underline{x}=(x_{0},x_{1},\dots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})
and the sum is over
rigid combinatorial types
\Gamma
of twice-quilted disks with
symplectic area
\leq E
. We will prove in Lemma
10.28

below that for any
d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})\geq 0

and any tuple of leaf labels
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}(L)^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}
,

(\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i}}-\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i+1}})^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})=(m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1},\leq E})^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})\mod q^{E}.

Composition using (
10.14
) produces a homotopy

\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E}:=\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{k-1},\lambda_{k},\leq E}\circ(\ldots\circ(\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\leq E}\circ\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\leq E}))

between
\phi_{12}\circ\phi_{01}
and
\phi_{02}
modulo terms
involving powers
q^{\geq E}
, that is,

\phi_{02}-(\phi_{12}\circ\phi_{01})=\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E}\mod q^{E}.

Taking the limit
E\to\infty
defines a
homotopy

\mathcal{T}_{02}:=\lim_{E\to\infty}\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E}

between
\phi_{02}
and
\phi_{12}\circ\phi_{01}
.
The limit exists because for any
E_{1}>E
,
\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E}=\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E_{1}}\operatorname{mod}q^{E}
, and therefore terms in
\mathcal{T}_{02}
with valuation
<E
are given by
\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\leq E}
.
Convergence,
that is, that
\mathcal{T}^{0}_{02}(1)
has coefficients with positive

q
-valuation, holds since any treed disk with no incoming
edges must, by stability, have a disk component with interior
markings, and therefore has positive area.
∎

Lemma 10.28
.

For an area bound
E>0
, let the interval
[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]
be as in (
10.45
).
Then,

\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\leq E}-\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i+1},\leq E}=m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1},\leq E})\mod q^{E}.

(10.47)

Configurations consisting of multiple twice-quilted disks attached to
an unquilted (dark shaded) disk by broken edges are not transversally
cut out, and they occur in the boundary of one-dimensional moduli
spaces. Indeed, our current definition of holomorphic twice quilted
disks require the ratios of radii (the surface version (
10.42
)
or the treed version (
10.44
)) to be equal for all twice
quilted components. This requirement may be viewed as a fiber product,
and the resulting moduli space is not transversally cut out.

In order to obtain transversality, the fiber products
(
10.42
), (
10.44
)
involved in the definition of the twice-quilted disks
must be perturbed, using
delay functions
, as
in Seidel
[
76
]
and Ma’u-Wehrheim-Woodward
[
52
]
.

In a twice-quilted disk type,
denote by

\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)\subset\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}(\Gamma)

the subset of boundary edges that lie below the inner quilting circle. In terms of Figure
10.11
, edges in
\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)
connect vertices with the darkest shading.
A
delay function
is a collection of maps

\tau_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}=(\tau_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma},\quad\tau_{\Gamma}=(\tau_{\Gamma,e})_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{R}^{\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)},

where
\Gamma
ranges over all rigid twice-quilted disk types with
d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})
interior leaves and
d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
non-root boundary leaves, and
\tau_{\Gamma}
satisfies the following:

(a)

(Locality) The map
\tau_{\Gamma}
depends only on the edge lengths
of edges in
\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)
,

(b)

(Zero edges) If for a curve
C\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}
the edge length of
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma)
is zero, then
\tau_{\Gamma,e}(C)=0
.

(c)

(Continuity) If
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a stratum containing an edge
e
of length zero, and
\Gamma_{0}
and
\Gamma_{1}
are open stratum in
\mathcal{M}_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}),d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}
obtained by collapsing the edge
e
and making
\ell(e)
non-zero respectively, then,

\tau_{\Gamma_{0}}|\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}=\tau_{\Gamma_{1}}|\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}.

(d)

(Infinite edges)
For any rigid twice quilted disk type
\Gamma^{\prime}
which has
\leq d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
boundary leaves and
\leq d({\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}})
interior leaves, with at least one of the inequalities being strict,
there exist delay maps

\tau_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}}=(\tau_{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},e})_{e}:\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{R}^{\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma^{\prime})}

such that the following holds:
If
\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}
is a stratum with a collection of edges of infinite length, that separate
\Gamma
into an unquilted type
\Gamma_{0}
with the darkest shading, and a set
\{\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{k}\}
of twice-quilted disk types connected to
\Gamma_{0}
by edges
e_{1},\dots,e_{k}
, so that
\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}
is a product

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}\xrightarrow{(\pi_{0},\dots,\pi_{k})}\mathcal{M}_{\Gamma_{0}}\times\prod_{i=1}^{k}\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma_{i}},

then for any edge
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}^{2}(\Gamma_{i})
,
i=1,\dots,k
, or
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma_{0})
and
i=0
,

\tau_{\Gamma,\Gamma_{i},e}\circ\pi_{i}=\tau_{\Gamma,e}.

(10.48)

Furthermore, for any
i=1,\dots,k
,

\tau_{\Gamma,e_{i}}=\text{constant}\quad\text{on }\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\Gamma}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}.

(10.49)

(e)

(Increasing condition)
Let
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
be paths in

\Gamma
starting from the root vertex
v_{0}
and such that neither of them is contained in the other. If
\gamma_{1}<\gamma_{2}
(in the sense that if, for
i=1,2
,
\gamma_{i}
is contained in the path from the root vertex
v_{0}
to a non-root leaf
e_{i}
, then
e_{1}
occurs before
e_{2}
), then

\sum_{e\in\gamma_{1}}\tau_{\Gamma,e}<\sum_{e\in\gamma_{2}}\tau_{\Gamma,e}.

(10.50)

We now define

delayed twice-quilted disks
where the fiber product is replaced by a perturbed version.
Define

\lambda_{\tau_{\Gamma}}:\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\operatorname{univ}}_{\Gamma}\to[0,\infty]^{\operatorname{Vert}_{12}(\Gamma)},\quad\lambda_{\tau_{\Gamma}}(C)_{v}:=(\lambda_{\Gamma}(C))_{v}+\sum_{e\in P(v_{0},v)}(\tau_{\Gamma}(C))_{e},

where
P(v_{0},v)
is the path in
\Gamma
from the root vertex
v_{0}
to
v
.
An unbalanced twice-quilted disk
C

is a
\tau
-delayed twice-quilted disk
if
\lambda_{\tau_{\Gamma}}(C)_{v}
is equal for all vertices
v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{12}(C)
containing an inner quilting circle
Q_{2}
,
and thus, the moduli space of
\tau
-delayed twice-quilted disks of type
\Gamma
is

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{0}}_{\Gamma,\tau}=\lambda_{\tau_{\Gamma}}^{-1}(\Delta),

equipped with a
delayed ratio of radius
map

\psi:\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{0}}_{\Gamma,\tau}\to\mathbb{R},\quad C\mapsto\lambda_{\tau_{\Gamma}}(v),

(10.51)

where
v\in\operatorname{Vert}^{12}(\Gamma)
can be any disk vertex containing an inner quilting circle. Delayed twice-quilted disks which have specific values of the delayed ratio of radii are denoted by

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{0}}_{\Gamma,\tau}:=\psi^{-1}(\lambda),\quad\mathcal{M}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma,\tau}:=\psi^{-1}([\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]).

Proof of Lemma
10.28
.

To define delayed holomorphic
twice-quilted disks, we choose perturbations close to the ones used
to define ordinary holomorphic twice-quilted disks. Given
perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}
for holomorphic twice quilted
disks, we take the delay functions to be small enough and define
coherent perturbations

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau}=(\mathfrak{p}_{012,\Gamma}^{\tau})_{\Gamma}

so that the moduli spaces

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{d},\quad\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{d},\quad\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i+1}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{d}

with expected dimension
d\leq 1
are transversally cut out, and
there are cobordisms of zero-dimensional moduli spaces

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i}}_{\Gamma}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{0}\sim\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau})_{0},\quad\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i+1}}_{\Gamma}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{0}\sim\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i+1}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau})_{0},

(10.52)

and

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012})_{0}\sim\mathcal{M}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma^{\prime},\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau})_{0}

(10.53)

for all types
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
.

We finally prove the identity required by the Lemma. For any

d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
, we consider input labels
(x_{1},\dots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})

and output label
x_{0}
, and we aim to prove

(\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\leq E}-\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i+1},\leq E})^{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})})=\\
(m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{T}_{02}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}}))^{\leq E,d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}).

(10.54)

We will show that (
10.54
)
is obtained by summing the
contributions from boundaries of one-dimensional moduli spaces

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau},\underline{x})_{1}
where
\Gamma
ranges over rigid types of
twice-quilted holomorphic disks with
d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})
boundary inputs and

\leq\frac{E}{k}
interior leaves as follows: A configuration
occurring in the boundary of

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau},\underline{x})_{1}

is

(a)

either a twice-quilted disk in

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{x})_{0}
or

\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i+1}}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{x})_{0}
; or

(b)

it has an unquilted disk with the lightest shading breaking off (as
in (A), (B) in Figure
10.13
); or

(c)

it has a collection of twice-quilted disks
breaking off (as in (C), (D) in Figure
10.13
).

Abbildung 10.13:
Configurations occurring in codimension one boundaries of
moduli spaces of delayed twice-quilted disks with a fixed
(generalized) ratio of radii. In (A) and (B), an unquilted disk
breaks off outside the outer quilting circle. In (C) and (D), a

A count of configurations of type (

), by the cobordism
in (

), is equal to the left hand side of

).
A configuration

of type

. By the cobordism (

occurring in

Finally, a configuration

u=(u_{0},\dots,u_{r})
of type (

) consists of an

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
-holomorphic disk, and a collection

(

Let

be the delayed ratio of radius (defined in (

)).
For degree reasons,

\{u_{1},\dots,u_{r}\}

was chosen so that there is at most one

For any

\psi(u_{j})=\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u}),

for some input labels
\underline{x}_{j}
. We then have the following Claim.

Claim 10.29
.

For

,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(u_{j})

Assuming Claim

,
there is a cobordism

\psi^{-1}(\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u}))\cap\mathcal{M}(u_{j})_{1}\sim\mathcal{M}^{{qq},\lambda_{i}}_{\Gamma_{j},\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau},\underline{x}_{j})_{0},

(10.55)

since the disjoint union of both the spaces in (

) form the boundary of

\overline{\mathcal{M}}(u_{j})_{1}\cap\{\psi\geq(\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u}))\}

(10.56)

In the case when

resp.

)

\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i}}(\underline{x}_{j}^{in})\quad\text{resp.}\quad\phi_{02}^{\lambda_{i+1}}(\underline{x}_{j}^{in}).

We conclude that the count of configurations

(u_{0},\dots,u_{r})

occurring in the boundary
of

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{{qq},[\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}]}_{\Gamma,\tau}(X,L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{012}^{\tau},\underline{x})_{1}

), and for

is equal to the count of terms

occurring in

(m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{T}^{\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}})^{\leq E,d({\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}})}
, which is the right hand side of (
