), finishing the proof of the Lemma.

It remains to prove Claim
10.29
. We consider the
case
j<k
, the proof of the other case,
j>k
, being similar. A map

v
occurring in the boundary of the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}(u_{j})_{1}
in

\psi^{-1}(\lambda_{i},\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u}))
is of one of the two
forms:

—

Either
v
has a collection of twice-quilted disks breaking
off, that is,
v=(v_{0},\dots,v_{r^{\prime}})
, where
v_{0}
is an unquilted
disk with darkest shading, the others are
\tau
-delayed
twice-quilted disks with one of them, say
v_{\ell}
, having index

0
. Since
u_{k}
is the only delayed twice-quilted disk of index

0
in
\psi^{-1}([\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}])
, we have that
v_{\ell}
is
the same as
u_{k}
. In particular,

\psi(v_{\ell})=\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C_{u})
. Suppose the root vertex
of
v_{k}
is connected to the root vertex of
v
by a path

\gamma
. By our assumption that
\psi(v)\in(\lambda_{i},\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}})
, we have

\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u})>\psi(v)=\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C)-\sum_{e\in\gamma}\tau_{\Gamma,e}(C),

(10.57)

where
the curve
C
is obtained by replacing the subdomain of
u_{j}
in
C_{u}
with the domain of
v
.
Note that (
10.48
) implies
\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C)=\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C_{u})
, and
(
10.49
) implies
\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C)=\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u})
. Therefore, (
10.57
) violates the increasing condition (
10.50
) at the curve
C
.

—

Otherwise,
v
has an unquilted disk bubbling off, that is
v=(v_{0},v_{1})
where
v_{0}
is twice-quilted and
v_{1}
is unquilted. Since
u_{k}
is the only delayed twice-quilted disk
of index
0
in
\psi^{-1}([\lambda_{i},\lambda_{i+1}])
, we have

\psi(u_{k})=\psi(v_{1})=\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C_{u})
, which can not lie in the interval

(\lambda_{i},\Lambda_{0}+\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u}))
, since the increasing condition (
10.50
) implies
that
\tau_{\Gamma,e_{j}}(C_{u})<\tau_{\Gamma,e_{k}}(C_{u})
.

This proves Claim
10.29
, and hence, also proves the Lemma.
∎

Corollary 10.30
.

For any two coherent convergent collections of perturbation data

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0},\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
the Fukaya algebras
CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})

and
CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1})
are homotopy equivalent via
unital convergent
A_{\infty}
morphisms

\phi:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}),\quad\psi:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0})

and unital convergent homotopies
h
,
g
satisfying

\phi\circ\psi-\operatorname{Id}=m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}(h),\quad\psi\circ\phi-\operatorname{Id}=m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}(g).

Beweis.

The
A_{\infty}
morphisms
\phi
,
\psi
are as constructed in the proof
of Proposition
10.18
, and the morphisms are unital
and convergent. We apply Theorem
10.26
taking

\mathfrak{p}_{2}=\mathfrak{p}_{0}
,
\phi_{01}=\phi
,
\phi_{12}=\psi
,
\phi_{02}=\operatorname{Id}
,
and obtain a unital convergent homotopy
h
. The homotopy
g
is obtained by
switching the role of

\mathfrak{p}_{0}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{1}
.
∎

10.7
Homotopy equivalence: unbroken to
broken

In this section, we show that the Fukaya algebra of the broken
manifold
\mathfrak{X}
is homotopy equivalent to the unbroken Fukaya algebra
on the manifold
X
. A rough outline of the proof is as follows. An
A_{\infty}
morphism from
an unbroken Fukaya algebra
CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})
, where
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
is a perturbation system on the neck-stretched manifold
X^{\nu}
,
to a broken one

CF^{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
is defined as a limit of
A_{\infty}
morphisms

\phi_{n}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+n})
as
n\to\infty
. The

A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi_{n}
is defined via a count of quilted
holomorphic disks in
(X,L)
. The existence of the limit relies on the
bijection between moduli spaces of broken and unbroken disks obtained
from the compactness and gluing theorems (Theorem

8.2
and Theorem
9.1
).

We use a breaking perturbation datum on neck-stretched manifolds, so
that the bijection between moduli spaces of broken and unbroken disks
holds. We recall from Definition
8.1
that given a
perturbation datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
on the broken manifold
\mathfrak{X}
, by
gluing on the neck we obtain a perturbation datum

\rho_{\nu}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
on any
X^{\nu}
. By Proposition

5.25
there is a stabilizing pair
(\mathfrak{J},\mathfrak{D})

consisting of a cylindrical almost complex structure
\mathfrak{J}
on
\mathfrak{X}

and a
\mathfrak{J}
-holomorphic cylindrical stabilizing divisor
\mathfrak{D}
, for
which the glued family
(J^{\nu},D^{\nu})
is a stabilizing pair on the
neck-stretched manifold
X^{\nu}
for all
\nu
. For the perturbation
datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
we use
\mathfrak{D}
as the stabilizing divisor and

\mathfrak{J}
as the background almost complex structure.

Proposition 10.31
.

Let
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
be a regular perturbation datum on
\mathfrak{X}
. For any

E_{0}>0
, there exists
\nu_{0}(E_{0})
such that the following holds.

(a)

There is a bijection between the zero-dimensional moduli spaces
of rigid broken maps and rigid unbroken maps:

\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{brok}}(\mathfrak{X},L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})_{0}^{<E_{0}}\simeq\mathcal{M}(X^{\nu},L,\rho_{\nu}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}))_{0}^{<E_{0}},\quad\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0}).

Furthermore, the moduli spaces of rigid quilted and twice-quilted
disks relating
X^{\nu}
and
X^{\nu+1}
with energy at most
E_{0}

are empty for
\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
.

(b)

For any
\nu\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, there exists
a regular perturbation datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
, and a perturbation
morphism
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
extending
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
and

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1}
such that for all
E_{0}>0
and for all

\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
, the (possibly curved)
A_{\infty}
homotopy
equivalence
induced by
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}

\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1})

is the identity map
modulo
q^{E_{0}}
. Similarly, there is a (possibly curved)
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence

\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})

that is the identity map
modulo
q^{E_{0}}
.
Furthermore, the homotopy
h_{\nu}
between

\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1}\circ\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}
and the identity,
and the homotopy
g_{\nu}
between

\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}\circ\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1}
and the identity
may be taken to be zero modulo
q^{E_{0}}
.

Beweis.

The proof of bijection of moduli spaces is a consequence of the
compactness and gluing theorems – Theorem
8.2

and Theorem
9.1
. By the gluing Theorem

9.1
, any regular
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
-disk
u:C\to\mathfrak{X}

can be glued to yield regular disks
u_{\nu}:C_{\nu}\to X^{\nu}
.
Conversely, any sequence
(u_{\nu})_{\nu}
of maps with area
\leq E_{0}

converges to a broken disk
u_{\infty}
. By surjectivity of gluing
in Theorem
9.1
, for large enough
\nu
,
u_{\nu}
is
contained in the image of the gluing map for
u_{\infty}
. Since the
moduli space
\mathcal{M}(\mathfrak{X},L)^{\leq E_{0}}_{0}
has a finite number of
points, the constant
\nu_{0}(E_{0})
can be chosen as the maximum
obtained from gluing each of the broken maps.

To prove the statements about quilted disks and part
(
b
), we construct regular perturbations and
perturbation morphisms on neck-stretched manifolds. A regular
perturbation datum is constructed by extending the breaking
perturbation datum. For any
E_{0}>0
and
\nu>\nu_{0}(E_{0})
, the
perturbation datum is defined as

\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\Gamma}:=\rho_{\nu}(\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}_{\Gamma})=(J^{\nu}_{\Gamma},F^{\infty}_{\Gamma})

(10.58)

if
E(\Gamma)\leq E_{0}
. For the other strata, a regular
perturbation
\mathfrak{p}^{\nu}_{\Gamma}
can be chosen using the
transversality result Theorem
6.27
for all
integers
\nu
.

For strata with low enough area, perturbation morphisms are
constructed using the breaking perturbation data. For

\nu\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,
\Gamma
satisfying
\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E(\Gamma))
,
the perturbation morphism is defined using the recipe in Remark

10.23
as

\mathfrak{p}^{\nu,\nu+1}_{\Gamma}=(J_{\Gamma}^{\nu,\nu+1},F^{\infty}_{\Gamma^{\prime}})

where
F^{\infty}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
is part of the perturbation data

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}=(\underline{J}^{\infty},\underline{F}^{\infty})
for broken disks,

\Gamma^{\prime}
is the type of broken disk obtained by forgetting the
quilting, and for any
z\in\mathcal{S}_{\Gamma}
,

J_{\Gamma}^{\nu,\nu+1}(z)
is the neck-stretched domain-dependent
almost complex structure from (
10.58
) with
neck-length parameter
\nu+d\circ\delta_{\Gamma}(z)
. The
perturbation morphisms are extended to higher strata so that they
are coherent and regular.

Perturbations for twice-quilted disks
are defined similarly: On low area strata, we use the
neck-stretched domain-dependent almost complex structure from
(
10.58
) with neck length parameter given by the
domain-dependent function
\nu+d\circ\delta_{\Gamma}(z)
. Here
\delta_{\Gamma}
is a version of
the “distance from the quilting circle” for twice-quilted disks from
[
17
, Definition
5.9]
.

We claim that for an area bound
E_{0}
, for sufficiently large

\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
, moduli spaces of holomorphic quilted and
twice-quilted disks are empty, with the exception of constant
quilted holomorphic disks with a single input and output with the
same label (as in Figure
10.8
). More precisely, we show that
the moduli space of non-constant quilted

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
-disks and twice-quilted disks
contributing to the homotopies
g_{\nu}
,
h_{\nu}
with area

\leq E_{0}
are empty if
\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
.
Suppose, by way of contradiction, that there is a sequence
u_{\nu}

of quilted
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
-disks with bounded area. Any
surface component of
u_{\nu}
is
J^{\nu^{\prime}}
-holomorphic for some

\nu^{\prime}\in[\nu,\nu+1]
. Therefore, after passing to a subsequence,
the surface part of the map
u_{\nu}
converge to a limit

J^{\infty}
-holomorphic broken map
u
. For large enough
\nu
, the
Maslov index
I(u_{\nu})
is
\nu
-independent, and therefore the
number of disk inputs is bounded. After passing to a subsequence,
we can assume that the disk input/output tuple
\underline{x}
and the type

\Gamma
is the same for all the quilted disks
u_{\nu}
. Since the

A_{\infty}
morphism counts quilted holomorphic disks with index
0
,
we may assume that the index of the quilted disks is zero:

i^{q}(\Gamma,\underline{x})=0
. The sequence
u_{\nu}
converges to a

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
-unquilted broken disk
u
of type
\Gamma_{\tau}

and input/output labels given by
\underline{x}
. Here
\Gamma_{\tau}
is
the type of a broken treed holomorphic map for which collapsing the
tropical edges yields a type
\Gamma^{\prime}
of a treed holomorphic map,
which is the same as the type obtained by forgetting the quilting
in
\Gamma
. The unquilted map
u
is stable in all cases except
the one contributing to the identity term, that is, when there is a
single input and output, and the map is constant on the surface and
tree part of the domain. In all other cases, the index of the
unquilted disk is one less (see (
10.36
)), and we have

i(\Gamma^{\prime},\underline{x})=-1
. Since collapsing tropical edges does not
affect the dimension, we have
i(\Gamma_{\tau},\underline{x})=-1
. The
existence of such a disk
u
is a contradiction, since

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
is a regular perturbation. The argument for
twice-quilted disks is similar.

Finally, the
A_{\infty}
morphisms
\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1},\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}

are defined by counting
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
-holomorphic quilted
holomorphic disks. The previous paragraph shows that if

\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
,

(\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1})^{1}=\operatorname{Id}\mod q^{E_{0}},\quad(\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1})^{d}=0\mod q^{E_{0}},d\neq 1,

and similarly for
\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}
.
The homotopies
h_{\nu}
,
g_{\nu}
are defined by counts of
twice-quilted disks, and therefore, they vanish modulo
q^{E_{0}}
if

\nu\geq\nu_{0}(E_{0})
.
∎

Corollary 10.32
.

Let
CF(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu},L)
and

CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty},L)
be the unbroken and broken Fukaya
algebras defined by the perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
,

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}
from Proposition
10.31
. The
structure maps
m^{d}_{\nu}
defining
CF(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu},L)
converge as

\nu\to\infty
to the structure maps
m^{d}_{\operatorname{brok}}
defining

CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty},L)
, in the sense that for any
E_{0}
there
exists
\nu(E_{0})
so that
m^{d}_{\nu}
agrees with
m^{d}_{\operatorname{brok}}
up to
terms divisible by
q^{E_{0}}
for
\nu>\nu(E_{0})
.

Completion of the second proof of Theorem
10.4
.

It suffices to prove that for any
E_{0}>0
that
homotopy-associativity holds up to order
q^{E_{0}}
. This follows
from Corollary
10.32
and Theorem
10.2
, since
the broken and unbroken composition maps are equal to up arbitrary
order, for sufficiently large neck length.
∎

Proposition 10.33
.

For any
\nu_{0}
, let
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\nu_{0}}
be the perturbation defined in
Proposition
10.31
. Then
there are convergent strictly unital
A_{\infty}
morphisms

\phi:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}})\to CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty}),\quad\psi:CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}})

such that
\psi\circ\phi
and
\phi\circ\psi
are
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalent by a convergent
A_{\infty}
homotopy.

Beweis.

The
A_{\infty}
morphisms
\phi
and
\psi
required by the Proposition
are defined to be the limits of the compositions of the
A_{\infty}
morphisms from Proposition
10.31

(
b
), namely,

\phi_{\nu}^{\nu+1}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1}),\quad\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}).

For any
E_{0}
, if
\nu\geq\nu^{\prime}(E_{0})
, the
A_{\infty}
morphisms

\psi_{\nu}^{\nu+1}
and
\psi_{\nu+1}^{\nu}
are equal to
\operatorname{Id}
modulo
q^{E_{0}}
. Therefore, there exist limits of the successive compositions:
Let

\phi_{n}:=\phi_{\nu_{0}+n-1}^{\nu_{0}+n}\circ\ldots\circ\phi_{\nu_{0}+1}^{\nu_{0}+2}\circ\phi_{\nu_{0}}^{\nu_{0}+1}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}+n})

for any choice of
\nu_{0}
. The limit

\phi=\lim_{n\to\infty}\phi_{n}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}})\to\lim_{n\to\infty}CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}+n})=CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})

exists, and similarly, for

\psi_{n}:=\psi_{\nu_{0}+1}^{\nu_{0}}\circ\ldots\circ\psi_{\nu_{0}+n}^{\nu_{0}+n-1}:CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}+n})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}}),

the limit

\psi=\lim_{n\to\infty}\psi_{n}:CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})\to CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu_{0}})

exists.
Since the composition of strictly unital morphisms is strictly unital,
the composition
\psi
is strictly unital mod terms divisible by
q^{E}

for any
E
, hence strictly unital. The limit map
\psi
resp.
\phi

is an
A_{\infty}
morphism whose domain resp. target is
CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})

because the composition maps in
CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})
converge to

CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
. The composition maps of

CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})
converge to those of

CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
by the bijection of the moduli spaces of
disks in Proposition
10.31

(
a
). Indeed, the bijection preserves the orientation

\epsilon(u)
, area
A(u)
, and holonomy
\operatorname{Hol}_{L}([\partial u])
since gluing
preserves these quantities.

We claim that
\phi
and
\psi
are homotopy equivalences. First, we inductively construct
homotopies
h_{n}
,
g_{n}
satisfying

\phi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}-\operatorname{Id}=m_{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}(h_{n}),\quad\psi_{n}\circ\phi_{n}-\operatorname{Id}=m_{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}(g_{n}).

Starting from
h_{n}
, the homotopy
h_{n+1}
is defined as follows:
By Proposition
10.31

(
b
),
there is a homotopy
H_{n+1}
satisfying

(\phi_{\nu_{0}+n}^{\nu_{0}+n+1}\circ\psi_{\nu_{0}+n+1}^{\nu_{0}+n})-\operatorname{Id}=m^{1}_{\mathcal{Q}}(H_{n+1})

that vanishes modulo
q^{E(n)}
for some
E(n)>0
. Define
H_{n+1}^{\prime}:=L_{\phi_{\nu_{0}+n}^{\nu_{0}+n+1}}(R_{\psi_{\nu_{0}+n+1}^{\nu_{0}+n}}(H_{n+1}))
by composing
H_{n+1}
with
A_{\infty}
morphisms as in (
10.15
), (
10.16
). Then,
H_{n+1}^{\prime}
is a homotopy from
\phi_{n+1}\circ\psi_{n+1}
to
\phi_{n}\circ\psi_{n}
that also vanishes modulo
q^{E(n)}
. The required homotopy
is then defined as

h_{n+1}:=H_{n+1}^{\prime}\circ h_{n},

where the composition of homotopies is as in (
10.14
), and
it has the property that

h_{n+1}=h_{n}\mod q^{E(n)}.

Since
E(n)\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
by Proposition
10.31

(
b
), the limit
h:=\lim_{n\to\infty}h_{n}
is well-defined. The homotopies
g_{n}
are defined
similarly, and the limit
g:=\lim_{n\to\infty}g_{n}
is well-defined.
Since the
A_{\infty}
algebra
CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\infty})
is a limit

\lim_{\nu\to\infty}CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})
(in the sense that composition maps converge),
and the
A_{\infty}
morphism
\psi_{n}
resp.
\phi_{n}
limits to
\psi
resp.
\phi
, we conclude

\phi\circ\psi-\operatorname{Id}=m_{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}(h),\quad\psi\circ\phi-\operatorname{Id}=m_{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}(g).

The
A_{\infty}
homotopies
h,g
are convergent
since each of them is the limit of convergent homotopies.
∎

10.8
Independence of perturbations in broken manifolds

In this Section, we show that broken Fukaya algebras are independent
of perturbations up to
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence,
where the perturbations are not required to be gluable.
For the proof of Theorem
1.6
(equivalently Proposition
10.33
) we used
X
-cylindrical almost complex structures(Definition
3.53
(
d
)) on broken manifolds, which were gluable. As a consequence of the result of this Section, broken Fukaya algebras can be defined using tamed almost complex structures on broken symplectic manifolds, called
\mathfrak{X}
-cylindrical almost complex
structures (Definition
5.9
).
Therefore, in applications, we need not distinguish between the spaces
X_{P}

and
X_{P}^{\omega}
, and the superscript
\omega
may
be dropped.

In order to interpolate between perturbations taking values in

X
-cylindrical and
\mathfrak{X}
-cylindrical almost complex structures, we
consider a generalized kind of almost complex structures called

\mathcal{G}
-cylindrical
. The underlying collection of inner products,
denoted by

g:=\{g_{Q}^{P}:\mathfrak{t}_{Q}\times\mathfrak{t}_{Q}\to\mathbb{R}:Q\subset P,\kern 5.0ptP\in\mathcal{P}^{0}\}

satisfies the
(Restriction)
condition, and
the collection of connection one-forms
(\alpha_{Q}^{P})_{Q\subset P}

satisfies the consistency condition (
3.23
) with respect to

g
.

As in the unbroken case, independence of broken Fukaya algebras of
perturbation data is proved by constructing an
A_{\infty}
morphism by
counts of quilted broken holomorphic disks which we now define. Given
regular perturbation data
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
on
\mathfrak{X}
that
take values in
\mathcal{G}
-cylindrical almost complex structures, a
perturbation morphism
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
connecting
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
and

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{1}
is as in the unbroken case in Definition
10.20

with the following differences: The domain-dependent almost complex structures are on
\mathfrak{X}
, and
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
is adapted to a path of stabilizing pairs

(\mathfrak{J}_{t},\mathfrak{D}_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
on
\mathfrak{X}
.

Definition 10.34
.

(Quilted broken holomorphic disks)
Given a perturbation morphism

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}=(\mathfrak{p}^{01}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}
on
\mathfrak{X}
, a
quilted
broken holomorphic disk
consists of a quilted treed disk
C
of
type
\Gamma
(Definition
10.11
), a tropical structure

\mathcal{T}
on
\Gamma
, and a
\mathfrak{p}^{01}_{\Gamma}
-holomorphic map

u:C\to\mathfrak{X}
with tropical structure
\mathcal{T}
, boundary mapping to the
Lagrangian submanifold
L\subset X_{P_{0}}
, and the map being
adapted to the family of broken divisors
\{\mathfrak{D}_{t}\}_{t}
as in
Definition
10.21
.

The following Proposition is analogous to Proposition

10.18
, and the proof of that Theorem carries over.

Proposition 10.35
.

Given regular perturbation data

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
, there is a comeager set
of regular perturbation morphisms extending
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
,

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
. Any such perturbation morphism induces a convergent unital
A_{\infty}
morphism

\phi=(\phi^{r})_{r\geq 0}:CF^{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})\to CF^{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1})

defined by counts of quilted broken holomorphic disks. The
A_{\infty}
morphism
\phi

is a
A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalence
and has zero-th composition map
\phi_{0}(1)
with positive

q
-valuation, that is

\phi^{0}(1)\in\Lambda_{>0}\langle{\hat{\mathcal{I}}(L)}\rangle
.

Kapitel 11
Potentials for semi-Fano toric surfaces

In this concluding Chapter,
we return to fill in the technical gaps in the
computations of disk potentials in semi-Fano toric manifolds in
Chapter
2
. The perturbations used to compute the disk
potentials in the cubic surface and the second Hirzebruch surface
depend not just on the domain treed curve,
but also on the tropical graph. We rigorously define this new kind
of perturbation, called
split perturbation
, and show that the
broken Fukaya algebras it produces are homotopy equivalent to the ones
we have defined so far. We also prove a technical result used in the
computations of disk potentials in semi-Fano toric manifolds in
Chapter
2
, namely that, the multiple cut of a semi-Fano toric
manifold, under some hypothesis, has the same disk potential as the
original smooth manifold.

11.1
Split perturbations

A split perturbation depends not just on the domain
curve but also on an accompanying tropical structure on it, recorded
by the
base tropical graph
.
The domain type for maps for split perturbations, denoted by
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
,
may have additional vertices than those in the base tropical graph

\Gamma
. However, for generic perturbations, for maps of index

\leq 1
, the domain type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
is equal to the base tropical graph
\Gamma
.

Definition 11.1
.

(Based curves) A

curve with base type
consists of

(a)

a stable treed disk
C
of type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
,

(b)

a
base tropical graph

\Gamma
,

(c)

and an edge collapse morphism
\kappa:{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\Gamma
that
necessarily collapses all disk edges
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}({\tilde{\Gamma}})

and treed segments
T_{e}
,
e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}({\tilde{\Gamma}})
, and
possibly some interior edges. (The map
\kappa
is an edge
collapse of graphs, and not of tropical graphs, because
{\tilde{\Gamma}}

does not have a tropical structure.)

The
type
of such a curve consists of the datum

{\tilde{\Gamma}}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
together with a root vertex

v_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
.
The
root vertex

v_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
of the base tropical graph is the vertex
containing the disk
components of
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
, that is,
\kappa(v)=v_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}}
for all

v\in\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}({\tilde{\Gamma}})
.

The moduli space of stable treed curves of type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
with base type

\Gamma
is denoted by
\mathcal{M}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}
, or simply
\mathcal{M}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}}}

when the context allows it. The topology on the space of based
curves is the standard topology on the space of stable curves with the
additional axiom that the base tropical graph is preserved in the
limit. This ends the Definition.

Morphsisms on treed disk types (Definition
6.2
) extend naturally to morphisms of treed disk types with base.

Definition 11.2
.

(Based graph morphisms)

The following morphisms are defined on types of curves with a base :

(a)

A based curve type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{1}\xrightarrow{\kappa_{1}}\Gamma
is obtained by
(Collapsing edges)

resp.
(Making an edge length/weight
finite/non-zero)
in the based curve type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\kappa_{0}}\Gamma
if there is a (Collapsing edges) resp. (Making an edge length/weight
finite/non-zero) morphism
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\kappa}}{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{1}
of treed curve types (see Definition
6.4
) and
\tilde{\kappa}\circ\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{1}
.
Note that the (Collapsing edges) morphism does not collapse an edge
e
in
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{0}
that is present in the base graph
\Gamma
.

(b)

A type with base

{\tilde{\Gamma}}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
is obtained by
(Cutting an
edge)
in
{\tilde{\Gamma}}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\kappa^{\prime}}\Gamma^{\prime}
if

—

{\tilde{\Gamma}}
is obtained by cutting an

e=(v_{+},v_{-})\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}},-}({\tilde{\Gamma}}^{\prime})
containing a breaking,

—

\Gamma^{\prime}
is obtained by identifying the vertices

\kappa(v_{+})
,
\kappa(v_{-})
in
\Gamma
.

In a split perturbation datum (Definition
11.4
), the
stabilizing divisor and background almost complex structure can be
chosen to be different for curve components corresponding to different
vertices of the base tropical graph. However,
the set of stabilizing pairs
ranging over all based curve types are required to satisfy the following coherence
condition, whose necessity arises from the coherence condition on split perturbation data.

Definition 11.3
.

(Coherence for stabilizing pairs)

(a)

(Inclusion of base tropical graphs) An
inclusion

i:\Gamma_{0}\to\Gamma_{1}
between base tropical graphs
is a map of graphs that
preserves the root vertex, edge directions, and polytope assignments on vertices.

(b)

(Coherence condition)
A collection of stabilizing pair assignments

(\underline{\mathfrak{J}},\underline{\mathfrak{D}})=(\mathfrak{J}_{v},\mathfrak{D}_{v})_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma),\Gamma}

for all vertices
v
in all base tropical graphs
\Gamma
is
coherent
, if
for any inclusion
i:\Gamma_{0}\to\Gamma_{1}
of base tropical graphs,

(\mathfrak{J}_{i(v)},\mathfrak{D}_{i(v)})=(\mathfrak{J}_{v},\mathfrak{D}_{v})\quad\forall v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma_{0}).

The coherence condition in Definition
11.3
implies that
the stabilizing pair for a root vertex is the same
for all base tropical graphs
\Gamma
.
Furthermore,

(\underline{\mathfrak{J}},\underline{\mathfrak{D}})
satisfies the above coherence condition for stabilizing
pairs if the condition is satisfied for inclusions of base tropical graphs
whose root vertex has a single edge incident on it.

Definition 11.4
.

(Split perturbation datum)

A domain-dependent perturbation
\mathfrak{p}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}
for a
based curve type
({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)
consists of
a stabilizing pair
(\mathfrak{J}_{v},\mathfrak{D}_{v})
corresponding to each vertex
v
of the base tropical graph
\Gamma
, and perturbations

J_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}:\mathcal{S}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\mathcal{J}^{\operatorname{cyl}}_{\omega_{\mathfrak{X}},\tau}(\mathfrak{X}),\quad F_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}:\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to C^{\infty}(L,\mathbb{R})

such that on
\mathcal{S}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\kappa^{-1}(v)}\subset\mathcal{S}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}
, namely the components corresponding to

\kappa^{-1}(v)
,
\mathcal{J}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}
is adapted to
(\mathfrak{J}_{v},\mathfrak{D}_{v})
. Here,
\mathcal{J}^{\operatorname{cyl}}_{\omega_{\mathfrak{X}},\tau}(\mathfrak{X})
is the space of
\omega_{\mathfrak{X}}
-tamed cylindrical almost complex structures on
\mathfrak{X}
(Definition
5.9
(
b
)).
A
coherent split perturbation datum

adapted to a coherent collection
(\underline{\mathfrak{J}},\underline{\mathfrak{D}})
of stabilizing pairs
is a collection

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}=(\mathfrak{p}_{({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)})_{({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)},

consisting of a perturbation datum

\mathfrak{p}_{({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)}=(J_{({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)},F_{({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)}),\quad J_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}:\mathcal{S}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\mathcal{J}^{\operatorname{cyl}}_{\omega_{\mathfrak{X}},\tau}(\mathfrak{X}),\quad F_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}:\mathcal{T}_{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to C^{\infty}(L,\mathbb{R})

for each based curve type
({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)
, that is coherent under the based graph morphisms of (Collapsing edges), (Making an edge length or weight finite/non-zero) and (Cutting edges) from Definition
11.2
, and the (Locality axiom) from Definition
6.4
.

The coherence condition for split perturbation data necessitates the coherence condition for stabilizing pairs as follows: Let
{\tilde{\Gamma}}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
be a based curve type, and suppose cutting a broken boundary edge
e
in
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
produces types whose base tropical graphs are
\Gamma_{0}
,
\Gamma_{1}
. Coherence of perturbations under cutting the edge
e
requires that stabilizing pairs satisfy the coherence condition corresponding to the inclusions
\Gamma_{0}\to\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{1}\to\Gamma
.

Remark 11.5
.

An important difference between ordinary perturbations (from
Definition
6.4
) on
\mathfrak{X}
and the split perturbations
in the above Definition
11.4
is that the split
perturbation datum
\mathfrak{p}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}
is not required to be
coherent under collapsing edges that are present in the base
tropical graph
\Gamma
. Indeed, for a based graph

{\tilde{\Gamma}}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\Gamma
, the

(Collapsing edges)
morphism
is only applicable on edges
e\in\operatorname{Edge}({\tilde{\Gamma}})\backslash\operatorname{Edge}(\Gamma)
.

Definition 11.6
.

(Broken maps with base)

Let
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
be a coherent split perturbation datum.

(a)

A
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
-holomorphic broken map with base
consists of

—

a based type
\kappa:{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\Gamma
,

—

and a broken map
u
whose domain is of type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
and
u

is
\mathfrak{p}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}
-holomorphic in the ordinary sense (as in
Definition
6.11
).
Furthermore, if

{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\operatorname{tr}}
is the tropical graph of
u
then
\kappa
factors
through
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\operatorname{tr}}
as

\kappa=\kappa_{1}\circ{\operatorname{tr}},\quad{\tilde{\Gamma}}\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}}{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\operatorname{tr}}\xrightarrow{\kappa_{1}}\Gamma,

where
\kappa_{1}
is a tropical edge collapse.

(b)

(Type) The
type
of an
broken map with base consists of the tropical graph
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
, the
tropical edge collapse map
{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\Gamma
, and the homology and
tangency data for the map
u
(as in the type of a broken map, see
Definition
6.13
). Whenever it is possible, the base
tropical type
\Gamma
is suppressed in the
notation.

(c)

(Tropical symmetry) For a type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
of a broken map
with base, the tropical symmetry group
T_{\operatorname{trop}}({\tilde{\Gamma}})
is the
symmetry group obtained by viewing
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
as a type of broken map
(by Definition
4.32
) and forgetting the base type

\Gamma
.

(d)

(Rigidity) The type
{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\Gamma
of a broken map with base
is
rigid
if
{\tilde{\Gamma}}
is rigid as a type of broken map. Thus
for a rigid type, the base tropical graph
\Gamma
is rigid, the
tropical graph
{\tilde{\Gamma}}_{\operatorname{tr}}
of the map is equal to
\Gamma
, and
the morphism
{\tilde{\Gamma}}\to\Gamma
only collapses treed components and
boundary nodes.

Split perturbations can be used to define broken Fukaya algebras.
Given a coherent split perturbation datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
on
\mathfrak{X}
, define the Fukaya algebra
as the graded vector space

CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}):=CF^{\operatorname{geom}}(L)\oplus\Lambda_{\geq 0}x^{\includegraphics[width]{greyt.pdf}}[1]\oplus\Lambda_{\geq 0}x^{{\includegraphics[width]{whitet.pdf}}}

with composition maps

m^{d}_{\operatorname{brok}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})=\sum_{x_{0},u\in{\mathcal{M}_{{\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma}(\underline{\mathfrak{p}},\underline{\eta},\underline{x})_{0}}}w_{s}(u)x_{0},

(11.1)

where
u
ranges over all rigid types
({\tilde{\Gamma}},\Gamma)
of broken maps
with base that have
d
inputs (see Definition
11.6

(
d
) for rigidity), and

w_{s}(u):=(-1)^{\heartsuit}(d_{\includegraphics[width]{black.pdf}}(\Gamma)!)^{-1}\operatorname{Hol}_{L}([\partial u])\epsilon(u)q^{A(u)},

(11.2)

where the symbols in (
11.2
) are as in (
10.18
),
and in particular the orientation is computed in the same way as for
broken map without base, see Remark
6.28
. As in the
case of broken maps (without base), for a one-dimensional component of
the moduli space, the configurations with a boundary edge of length
zero constitute a fake boundary, whereas those with a broken boundary
edge constitute the true boundary of the moduli space. Setting the
counts of the maps occurring in the true boundary to zero yields the

A_{\infty}
associativity relations, and we conclude that
CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})

is an
A_{\infty}
algebra.

Broken Fukaya algebras defined by counts of broken maps with base are
independent of the choice of perturbation up to homotopy equivalence.
The proof is similar to the proof of independence of perturbations for
unbroken Fukaya algebras shown in Proposition
10.18
.
Assuming that the stabilizing divisors have the same degree, the proof
is by constructing an
A_{\infty}
morphism by counts of quilted broken
holomorphic disks with base. These are holomorphic disks with base,
with the additional datum of a quilting circle on disks. Split
perturbations differ in the sense that they are not coherent under
collapsing the edges in the base tropical graph. The extension is
straightforward because quilting phenomena take place on disk
components, and all disk components are collapsed into a single vertex in the base tropical graph.
We obtain the following result, which is an extension of Proposition

10.35
.

Proposition 11.7
.

(Independence of perturbations, split version) Suppose

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1}
are regular split perturbation data that
are defined using coherent collections of stabilizing pairs
(\underline{\mathfrak{J}}^{0},\underline{\mathfrak{D}}^{0})
and
(\underline{\mathfrak{J}}^{1},\underline{\mathfrak{D}}^{1})
,
which are connected by a path of a coherent collection of stabilizing pairs

\{(\underline{\mathfrak{J}}^{t},\underline{\mathfrak{D}}^{t})\}_{t\in[0,1]}
.
There exists a coherent perturbation
datum
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{01}
which induces a convergent unital
A_{\infty}
morphism (as in (
10.9
))

\phi_{01}:CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})\to CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{1})

which is a homotopy equivalence.

In fact, Fukaya algebras defined using ordinary perturbation data are

A_{\infty}
homotopy equivalent to those defined using split perturbation
data:

Proposition 11.8
.

Let
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
be a regular coherent perturbation datum for the
broken manifold
(\mathfrak{X},L)
and let

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}=(\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}
be a collection of regular
coherent split perturbation data for all rigid tropical graphs

\Gamma
.
Then the broken Fukaya algebras
CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})
,
CF_{\operatorname{brok}}(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime})
are homotopy equivalent.

Beweis.

The result follows from the homotopy equivalence of any two broken
Fukaya algebras defined by split perturbations, and the fact that a
non-split perturbation may be regarded as a split perturbation in
the following way: Given a perturbation
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
without base, we
define a split perturbation
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
by forgetting the base
tropical graph. The resulting curve counts defining the composition
maps are the same for
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
. Indeed, a

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
-holomorphic map of type
\Gamma
corresponds to a unique

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}
-holomorphic map of type
\Gamma\xrightarrow{{\operatorname{tr}}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{tr}}

where
{\operatorname{tr}}
is the tropicalization map; the uniqueness follows from
the fact that since
\Gamma
is rigid, there is no other tropical
graph that can be obtained by edge collapses.
∎

11.2
Potentials for semi-Fano toric surfaces

Recall that in Section
2.2
we counted disks in a
cubic surface by deforming the symplectic form on the cubic surface to
that of a
semi-Fano
toric surface.
A complex manifold
X
is semi-Fano if the first Chern
number
c_{1}(TX|S)
is non-negative on any holomorphic curve

S\subset X
. In this section, we show that the disk potential of a
toric Lagrangian in a semi-Fano toric surface is well-defined if we
use a perturbation datum for which the almost complex structure is
close enough to a divisor-preserving almost complex structure – these
are almost complex structures for which the torus-invariant divisors
are holomorphic. We also show that under some homological hypotheses,
the potential is preserved by a multiple cut on a semi-Fano toric
surface. These results were used in
Sections
2.1
and

2.2

to obtain the disk potential by counting broken
disks.

Definition 11.9
.

Let
X
be a toric symplectic manifold. A tamed almost complex
structure
J
on
X
is called
divisor-preserving
if all toric
divisors
Y
of
X
are
J
-holomorphic, that is,
J(TY)=TY
.

We may associate a disk potential
W\in\Lambda_{\geq 0}
to a non-regular almost complex structure
J_{0}
,
if
W
is the disk potential for any regular perturbation taking values in a small enough neighborhood of
J_{0}
.

Definition 11.10
.

(Potential for a non-regular almost complex structure) Suppose

J_{0}
is a compatible almost complex structure on a symplectic
manifold
(M,\omega)
and
L\subset M

is
a Lagrangian with a brane
structure. Let
\underline{F}=(F_{\Gamma})_{\Gamma}
be a coherent regular
domain-dependent Morse function on
L
. The manifold
(X,J_{0},L)

with Morse datum
\underline{F}
has potential

W_{0}(J_{0})\in\Lambda_{>0}

(11.3)

if the following holds: Given an area level
E_{0}
, there exists

\epsilon>0
such that for any coherent regular perturbation

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}=(\underline{J},\underline{F})
such that
\underline{J}
maps to an

\epsilon
-neighborhood of
J_{0}
,

m^{0}_{CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})}(1)=W_{0}1^{\includegraphics[width]{blackt.pdf}}\mod q^{E_{0}}.

Proposition 11.11
.

(Potential on semi-Fano toric manifolds)

Let
X
be a semi-Fano symplectic toric manifold and
L\subset X

is a toric Lagrangian. Let
J_{0}
be a divisor-preserving almost
complex structure, and let
\underline{F}
be a generic coherent
domain-dependent Morse function on
L
. The potential for the
manifold
(X,J_{0},L)
is well-defined in the sense of Definition

11.10
.

Beweis.

As a first step, we define the element

m^{0}_{J_{0}}(1)\in\Lambda_{\geq 0}\langle{\operatorname{Crit}(F)}\rangle
for the
divisor-preserving almost complex structure
J_{0}
.

Claim
.

There is an element
m^{0}_{J_{0}}(1)\in\Lambda_{\geq 0}\langle{\operatorname{Crit}(F)}\rangle
such
that for any area level
E_{0}
there exists
\epsilon>0
such that for
any coherent regular perturbation
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}
that is
\epsilon
-close
to
J_{0}
,

m^{0}_{CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}})}(1)=m^{0}_{J_{0}}(1)\mod q^{E_{0}}.

To prove the Claim, let us assume the opposite, that is, there is
a sequence of coherent regular perturbations
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
that
converge uniformly to the constant
J_{0}
, and such that

m^{0}_{CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu})}(1)\neq m^{0}_{CF(L,\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1})}(1)\mod q^{E_{0}}

for all
\nu
. Choose a regular perturbation morphism

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}=(\underline{J}^{\nu,\nu+1},\underline{F})

with end points
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
,
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1}
, such that the
sequence
\underline{J}^{\nu,\nu+1}
converges uniformly to the constant

J_{0}
as
\nu\to\infty
. Since the potentials are unequal for

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}
and
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu+1}
, there is a

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
-quilted disk
u_{\nu}
with no inputs, having
index zero, and area at most
E_{0}
, which contains a non-constant
quilted surface component
S_{v}
. Indeed, the boundary of the
one-dimensional part of the moduli space of quilted disks with no
inputs consists of quilted disks with a broken edge
e
(that is,

\ell(e)=\infty
), and the only configurations where the map is
constant on the quilted disk are those counted in

m^{0}_{CF(L,P^{\nu})}(1)
and
m^{0}_{CF(L,P^{\nu+1})}(1)
.

We now rule out the appearance of quilted disks by a dimension argument. The quilted disk
u_{\nu}
from the previous paragraph is of one of the forms shown in Figure
11.1
. A subsequence of the sequence
u_{\nu}
converges
to a
J_{0}
-holomorphic treed disk
u_{\infty}
of index
-1
. The
limit
u_{\infty}
also has a broken edge, and let
u_{\infty}^{i}
,

i=0,1,\cdots
, be the maps obtained by cutting the broken edges in

u_{\infty}
. One of these maps, say
u_{\infty}^{j}
, has index
-1
and
the map on the surface part is non-constant. The surface component
in
u_{\infty}^{j}
is
J_{0}
-holomorphic, and so, it consists of

—

disks that have positive isolated intersections with the toric
divisors, and so, have Maslov index at least
2
;

—

and spheres that have
c_{1}\geq 0
by the semi-Fano condition.

Consequently, if
u_{\infty}^{j}
does not have inputs, the index

i(u_{\infty}^{j})\geq 0
, which rules out configurations of the form
(b) in Figure
11.1
for large enough
\nu
. Suppose an
input leaf of
u_{\infty}^{j}
is the output of a treed disk

u_{\infty}^{k}
as in Figure
11.1
(a). By the same
reasoning used for
u_{\infty}^{j}
the surface part of
u_{\infty}^{k}
has
Maslov index at least two, and so, the output is necessarily the maximum
point
x^{\includegraphics[width]{blackt.pdf}}
. Therefore, in Figure
11.1
(a), any
input to the
(-1)
-index disk
u_{\infty}^{j}
is
x^{\includegraphics[width]{blackt.pdf}}
, and
consequently, the disk
u_{\infty}^{j}
does not exist for degree
reasons since the surface component has Maslov index at least
2
.
Therefore the configuration (a) is also ruled out for large

\nu
. Thus the Claim is proved.

Finally, we show that
m^{0}_{J_{0}}(1)
is a multiple of
x^{\includegraphics[width]{blackt.pdf}}
: For
any sequence of regular perturbations

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu}=(\underline{J}^{\nu},\underline{F})
that uniformly converge to

(J_{0},\underline{F})
, and a sequence of treed disks
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\nu}
disks

u_{\nu}
with no inputs, of index zero, and uniformly bounded area,
a subsequence converges to a
(J_{0},\underline{F})
-disk
u_{\infty}
of index

0
. By the semi-Fano condition the Maslov index of
u_{\infty}
is
at least
2
,
and since there are no inputs, a dimension count dictates
that
x^{\includegraphics[width]{blackt.pdf}}
is the only possible output.
∎

Abbildung 11.1:
Codimension one strata in a moduli space of quilted disks with no inputs.

Proposition 11.12
.

For a semi-Fano toric symplectic manifold, the potential
W(J_{0})

does not depend on the choice of divisor-preserving almost complex
structure
J_{0}
.

Outline of proof.

Assume the opposite, so that there is a path
\{J_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
of divisor-preserving almost complex structures on which the terms in the potential with area level

\leq E_{0}
are not
t
-independent. Then, there is a sequence of
quilted disks with area
\leq E_{0}
that converge to a

J_{t}
-holomorphic disk of index
-1
for some
t\in[0,1]
. The
existence of such a disk is ruled out as in the proof of Proposition

11.11
.
∎

The following Proposition gives a sufficient condition under which the potential is not altered by multiple cutting of a semi-Fano toric surface.

Proposition 11.13
.

(When breaking does not alter the potential)
Let
X
be a semi-Fano toric surface,
\mathcal{P}
a toric multiple cut,
and
L\subset\overline{X}_{P_{0}}
a toric Lagrangian in a component

\overline{X}_{P_{0}}
of the broken manifold
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Let
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
be a
broken divisor-preserving tamed almost complex structure on

\mathfrak{X}
. Suppose for any broken
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
-holomorphic disk
u
in
\mathfrak{X}
,

I(u_{{\operatorname{glue}}})\geq 2
. Then the potential for
(\mathfrak{X},\mathfrak{J}_{0},L)
is
well-defined and is the same as the toric potential for
X
equipped
with a divisor preserving almost complex structure.

Beweis.

We argue by contradiction, using Gromov compactness.
Let
J_{0}^{\nu}
be the divisor preserving almost complex structure
obtained from
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
by gluing the cylindrical ends of
\mathfrak{X}
.
Following the proof of Proposition
11.11
, if the
Proposition were not true, there would exist

—

a sequence of regular perturbations
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\nu}
on
X^{\nu}

that converge to the constant broken almost complex structure

J_{0}
,

—

perturbation morphisms
\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
that also
converge to
J_{0}
,

—

a sequence
u_{\nu}
of

\underline{\mathfrak{p}}^{\nu,\nu+1}
-quilted disks of index zero, uniformly
bounded area, each having a broken edge
e
(that is,
\ell(e)=\infty
), and a quilted surface component on which the map is non-constant.

Consequently, after passing to a subsequence, the sequence

\{u_{\nu}\}_{\nu}
converges to a
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
-holomorphic broken treed disk

u_{\infty}
of index
-1
. Indeed, this is the only case to
consider, since in the proof of Proposition
11.11

we have already ruled out quilted disks of the above form that
converge to a
J^{\nu}_{0}
-holomorphic disk for a finite
\nu
. The
quilted disks in the sequence
\nu
are of one of the two forms
shown in Figure
11.1
. Copying the arguments from the proof of
Proposition
11.11
, each of the cases is ruled out
using the fact that for a broken
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
-holomorphic disk
u
can not have
index
i^{\operatorname{brok}}(u)=-1
as follows: By Proposition
6.21
(
b
), the glued type has the same index, that is,

i^{\operatorname{brok}}(u)=i(u_{\operatorname{glue}})=-1
, which is not possible, since by the hypothesis,
the Maslov
index
I(u_{\operatorname{glue}})
is at least two.
∎

Abbildung 11.2:
A broken disk with Maslov index
0
in the second Hirzebruch surface with the multiple cut
\mathcal{P}_{1}
from Example
2.5
.

Remark 11.14
.

(A counter-example) In Example
2.5
we saw that there is
a multiple cut
\mathcal{P}_{2}
on the second Hirzebruch surface
X:=F_{2}
for
which the potential of the broken manifold
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}
differs
from that of the unbroken one
X
. The broken manifold

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}
does not satisfy the hypothesis of Proposition

11.13
because there is a broken disk
u
(shown
in Figure
11.2
) whose glued Maslov index
I(u_{\operatorname{glue}})

is
0
. The map
u
contains a disk of Maslov index
4
and two
spheres with self-intersection
-1
.

In the following result, we verify that the multiple cut
\mathcal{P}
on the
cubic surface given in Figure
11.3
satisfies the
hypothesis of Proposition
11.13
, and use it to
conclude that the multiple cut preserves the potential of the toric
Lagrangian in the cubic surface.

Abbildung 11.3:
Left: Multiple cut
\mathcal{P}
on the cubic surface
X
. Right: Components of the broken cubic surface
\mathfrak{X}
.

Proposition 11.15
.

In the multiply cut cubic surface
\mathfrak{X}
as in Figure

11.3
equipped with a divisor-preserving
domain-dependent almost complex structure
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
and a toric
Lagrangian
L\subset\overline{X}_{P_{0}}
, for any
\mathfrak{J}_{0}
-holomorphic
broken disk
u
, the glued Maslov index
I(u_{{\operatorname{glue}}})
is at least

2
. Consequently, the multiple cut of Figure

11.3
preserves the disk potential of the toric
Lagrangian
L
.

Beweis.

The proof is combinatorial and proceeds by analyzing various cases.
We first consider disks whose tropical nodes do not map to orbifold
singularities, and prove that their glued Maslov index is at least

2
. For a rigid broken map
u:C\to\mathfrak{X}
of type
\Gamma
, the
Maslov index of the glued type
\Gamma_{\operatorname{glue}}
is

I(\Gamma_{\operatorname{glue}})=\sum_{v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)}\overline{I}(u_{v}),\quad\overline{I}(u_{v}):=I(u_{v})-\sum_{e\in\operatorname{Edge}_{{\operatorname{trop}}}(\Gamma):e\ni v}2\mu_{e,v},

(11.4)

where
\mu_{e,v}
is the sum of the intersection multiplicities with
all the relative divisors at the lift of the node
w_{e}
on
C_{v}
,
and
I(u_{v})
is the Maslov index of the disk/sphere
u_{v}
. Indeed,
(
11.4
) follows from (
6.34
) by
observing that
\overline{I}(u_{v})=I_{\operatorname{adj}}(u_{v})-2|\operatorname{Edge}_{\operatorname{trop}}(\Gamma_{v})|

where
\operatorname{Edge}_{\operatorname{trop}}(\Gamma_{v})
is the set of tropical edges incident
on
v
(see also the related Remark
6.23
). Via a
case-by-case analysis of the components of the map, we will show
that
I(\Gamma_{\operatorname{glue}})\geq 2
. In the sequel, we say that

v\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
is a
descendent
of
w\in\operatorname{Vert}(\Gamma)
if
there is an edge between
v
and
w
, and
v
is further from the
root than
w
.

Case 1
: If the map
u_{v}
does not map to a toric divisor
of
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P(v)}
then
\overline{I}(u_{v})\geq 0
. Indeed, in this case

\overline{I}(u_{v})
is equal to the number of intersections of the map

u_{v}
with non-boundary toric divisors counted with multiplicity,
which is a non-negative integer. An inspection of the multiply cut
cubic surface
\mathfrak{X}
tells us that this case covers all maps
u_{v}

where
\operatorname{codim}(P(v))>0
.

Case 2
: Suppose
P(v)
is top-dimensional,

P(v)\neq P_{0}
, and
u_{v}
maps to a non-boundary toric divisor

Y\subset\overline{X}_{P(v)}
. Then
Y
is a
(-1)
-curve and one of the
ends of
Y
intersects a non-boundary toric divisor

Y_{1}\subset\overline{X}_{P(v)}
. Therefore,
\overline{I}(u_{v})=0
.

Case 3
: Suppose
P(v)=P_{0}
and
u_{v}
maps to a long
divisor, say
D_{1}^{\prime}
. In this case
\overline{I}(u_{v})
is negative, but we
show that its contribution is cancelled by positive contributions
from some descendent vertices of
v
. For the moment, we assume

u_{v}
is a simple cover of
D_{1}^{\prime}
. Then
v
has a descendent
v_{1}

such that

(Case A)
P(v_{1})=P_{1}
or
P_{01}
or  (Case B)

P(v_{1})=P_{4}
or
P_{04}
.

Since Cases (A) and (B) are symmetric, we only consider (A). If

P(v_{1})=P_{1}
,
u_{v_{1}}
intersects either
D_{1}^{\prime\prime}
or
E_{1}^{\prime}
, both of
which are non-relative toric divisors of
\overline{X}_{P_{1}}
; and if

P(v_{1})=P_{01}
,
u_{v_{1}}
has an intersection with the thickening
of
D_{1}^{\prime}\cap D_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, which is a non-boundary toric divisor of

\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P_{01}}
. In both cases
I(u_{v_{1}})=2
, which cancels the
negative contribution
I(u_{v})=-2
. In case
u_{v}
is a
k
-cover of

D_{1}^{\prime}
, then a similar cancellation argument applies, using
descendent vertices of
v
.

So far, we have shown that the sum of
\overline{I}(u_{v})
over all vertices
is non-negative. We now show that the sum is positive. Consider a
disk component
v\in\operatorname{Vert}_{\includegraphics[width]{white.pdf}}(\Gamma)
on which the map is
non-constant. The disk
u_{v}
either intersects a long divisor, in
which case
\overline{I}(u_{v})\geq 2
; otherwise it intersects the relative
divisor, say
\overline{X}_{P_{01}}
. In the latter case, there is a
descendent vertex
v_{1}
of
v
for which
\overline{I}(u_{v_{1}})\geq 2

using the same argument as in Case 3. This finishes the proof in the
case when the tropical nodes of the broken disk
u
do not map to
orbifold singularities.

In the case when a tropical node on a component
u_{v}
maps to an
orbifold singularity in
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P(v)}
, we consider a toric
resolution
\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{v}
of
\overline{\mathfrak{X}}_{P(v)}
and a lift

\tilde{u}_{v}:C_{v}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{X}}_{v}
. The formula
(
11.4
) is applicable if the Maslov index
I(u_{v})

and the sum of intersection multiplicities
\mu_{e,v}
are replaced
by the corresponding quantities for the lift
\tilde{u}_{v}
. The other
arguments in the proof now carry over. In fact, the components
u_{v}

mapping to orbifold points are covered by Case 1, since in the
multiply cut cubic surface, toric divisors in
X_{P(v)}
do not
contain orbifold singularities in their closure.

We have shown that in the multiply cut cubic surface
\mathfrak{X}
, the
glued Maslov index of any broken disk is at least
2
. Therefore, we
may apply Proposition
11.13
on
\mathfrak{X}
and
conclude that the potential of the toric Lagrangian
L
in the cubic
surface is preserved under the multiple cut
\mathcal{P}
. This finishes
the proof of Proposition
11.15
.
∎

Remark 11.16
.

A curious reader may wonder whether the potential has the same terms
if the Lagrangian is in some other top-dimensional piece of the
multiply cut cubic surface. The potential has the same terms as the
unbroken case when
L
is a toric Lagrangian in any top-dimensional
piece
\overline{X}_{P_{i}}
,
i=0,\dots,9
in the broken manifold. Indeed,
the proof of Proposition
11.15
can be replicated,
and there are no disks of Maslov index less than
2
in each of the
cases. However, if
L\subset\overline{X}_{P_{i}}
,
i\neq 0
, the disks
can not be counted in a straightforward way, since some of the
broken disks contain components that are multiple covers of

(-1)
-spheres. For example, for the disk
u
in Figure

11.4
the gluing
u_{\operatorname{glue}}
has homology class

\delta_{E_{5}}
. The component
u_{v_{3}}
in
u
is homologous to a
double cover
2[D_{1}^{\prime}]
; the count of such a configuration is
-{\tfrac{1}{2}}

by the Graber-Pandharipande multiple cover formula
[
12
]

(and for which we provide an alternate proof in
[
82
]

without using virtual localization). Therefore, after
accounting for the multiplicity of the edge
e
, the map
u

contributes
-1
to the count. There is another broken disk
u^{\prime}
in
the same homology class, where the sphere
u_{v_{3}}
is replaced by
two spheres
u_{v_{3}}
,
u_{v_{3}^{\prime}}
, each of homology class
[D_{1}^{\prime}]

(see Figure
11.5
); there are two broken maps of this type
obtained by interchanging the sub-trees attached at the vertices

v_{3}
,
v_{3}^{\prime}
, adding
+2
to the curve count. Together, the two
types of maps give a contribution of
+1
, which is the contribution
of a disk of class
\delta_{E_{5}}
in the unbroken case.

Abbildung 11.4:
A broken map
u
whose gluing is in the class
\delta_{E_{5}}
, and which contains a multiple cover. The node
(v_{1},v_{2})
(whose
v_{1}
-end maps to an orbifold singularity) is similar to the node in Example
6.24
(
b
).

Abbildung 11.5:
The graph of a broken map
u^{\prime}
whose gluing is in the class

\delta_{E_{5}}
, but which does not have any component that is
homologous to a multiple cover.
